# Calculations, formulas & tables

> Generated by `elcalc markdown` from the calculation core (version 1.11.0).
> No warranty – check standard values against the edition in force before use.

## Project lists

### Consumer list & cable sizing

*Power balance NN/NEA/USV, protection, cross-section, ΔU per consumer* — `consumer_list`

```text
Chain per consumer (as in the Excel tool "Leistungsdaten_Kabelauslegung"):
S = P / cos φ   I_B = S / (k · U), k = √3 (400 V) or 1
Protection: MSS/SelMod adjustable (I_N = I_B, sized on I_B), MCB/gG next rating (sized on I_N)
F_tot = f1 · f2 · f3 · f4   I_r,min = I_B or I_N / F_tot → cross-section per DIN VDE 0298-4 / IEC 60364-5-52
I_Z = I_r · F_tot   check I_B ≤ I_N ≤ I_Z (MCB/gG also I_2 ≤ 1.45 · I_Z)
ΔU = k · I_B · L · (R' cos φ + X' sin φ), k = √3 or 2, R' = ρ(θ)/A, X' by cross-section
Columns per VDI 3814 Part 4.1 Tab. 6 (ELT consumer list). f1/f2 empty = calculated from θ_a and grouping.
```

**Project settings:** Permitted voltage drop [%], Conductor temperature for ΔU [°C], Increase cross-section for ΔU automatically

**Columns per row:** Plant/device, BAS / identifier, Trade, System/panel, Location/room, Quantity, Connection ELT/MSR, Supply, Rated voltage, Rated power [kW], cos φ, Simultaneity factor, Redundant (standby), Protective device, Installation method, Insulation, Ambient temperature [°C], f1 temperature (manual), Grouping – arrangement, Grouped circuits, f2 grouping (manual), f3 harmonics, f4 additional factor, Cable length [m], Fixed cross-section

**Results per row:** Apparent power S [kVA], S with simultaneity, qty [kVA], Design current I_B [A], Protective device I_N [A], Device, Sized on, Total factor F, Min. rating I_r,min [A], Area by ampacity [mm²], Selected cross-section [mm²], Table rating I_r [A], Current capacity I_Z [A], I_B ≤ I_N ≤ I_Z, Voltage drop ΔU [V], Voltage drop ΔU [%]

**Totals:** Total active power [kW], Total apparent power [kVA], Total current (400 V) [A], Upstream gG fuse (proposal) [A], S mains (NN) [kVA], S standby (NEA) [kVA], S UPS [kVA], S connection ELT [kVA], S connection MSR [kVA], S 400 V [kVA], S 230 V [kVA], S 24 V [kVA], Consumer rows, Thereof not OK, Largest voltage drop [%]

### BACS data point list (VDI 3814)

*Count AI/AO/BI/BO/COM, modules, BAS addresses per VDI 3814-4.1* — `ga_points`

```text
BACS function list – input/output functions (VDI 3814 Part 3.1: analog/binary input AI/BI, analog/binary output AO/BO, communicative data points COM) from component templates.
User address (BAS) per VDI 3814 Part 4.1: site_building_ISP_trade_plant+no_component+no_function+no, e.g. K_HG_01_L_VKA01_ZUV01_SB_01. Trade (Tab. 1), plant (Tab. 2), component (Tab. 3), function identifier (Tab. 4).
Modules = ⌈count · (1 + reserve) / channels per module⌉. Fault/danger messages as NC contacts (VDI 3814-3.1).
Templates reflect planning practice and must be adapted per project (component code can be overridden).
```

**Project settings:** Site, Building, Automation focal point (ISP), Separator, Spare [%], Channels per AI module, Channels per AO module, Channels per BI module, Channels per BO module, Price per physical data point [€ / CHF], Price per communicative data point [€ / CHF]

**Columns per row:** Component plain text, Plant (VDI 3814-4.1 Tab. 2), Plant number, Component template, Component code (empty = template), Component number (start), Quantity

**Results per row:** AI, AO, BI, BO, COM (bus), Physical data points, First user address, Functions

**Totals:** Physical data points, Communicative data points, Analog input AI, Analog output AO, Binary input BI, Binary output BO, Physical incl. spare, AI modules, AO modules, BI modules, BO modules, Data point cost estimate [€]

### Cable tray: fill, width and load

*Enter cables → tray width with reserve, fill, weight, grouping* — `cable_tray`

```text
Space per cable d² (square around the cable), sum × (1 + reserve); guide value reserve ≈ 30 %.
Tray width = smallest standard width (50 … 600 mm) with B · H ≥ demand; fill = Σ d² / (B · H).
Cable load q = Σ n · m' (kg/m), F = q · 9.81 N/kg; design load per VDE 0639: 0.028 N/(m·mm²) × B · H.
Enter the permissible load for the support spacing from the manufacturer's load diagram (spacing preferably ≤ 1.5 m).
Grouping factor of the power cables per IEC 60364-5-52: single layer on perforated tray or ladder, otherwise bunched.
Cable data (d, weight) from data sheets: NYM (Faber), NYY-J (Waskönig+Walter), J-Y(St)Y and LiYCY (Lapp), Cat 7 (DIGITUS) – enter d and weight for other types. Method: OBO Bettermann KTS guide “system selection”.
```

**Project settings:** Tray type, Side height (usable height), Space reserve [%], Support spacing [m], Permissible load from load diagram [kN/m], Separator power/data

**Columns per row:** Designation, Cable type, Quantity, Outer diameter (if different) [mm], Weight (if different) [kg/km], Kind

**Results per row:** d [mm], Space n · d² [mm²], Load [kg/m], Width side by side [mm], Kind, Recognised as

**Totals:** Tray width (standard) [mm], Number of trays, Fill without reserve [%], Occupancy with reserve [%], Space Σ n · d² [mm²], Demand with reserve [mm²], Cable weight [kg/m], Cable load per tray [kN/m], Design load VDE 0639 [kN/m], Power cables, Data/control cables, Width of power cables side by side [mm], Grouping factor power cables

## Electrical engineering

### Basics

#### Power, current and voltage

*P, Q, S, I or U for DC, 1~ and 3~ – also motors with cos φ and η* — `power`

```text
P_in = k · U · I · cos φ    P_out = P_in · η    k = 1 (DC, 1~), √3 (3~, U = line voltage)
Current: I = P_out / (k · U · cos φ · η)    Voltage: U = P_out / (k · I · cos φ · η)
S = k · U · I    Q = S · sin φ    φ = arccos(cos φ)    U₀ = U / √3    1 hp = 745.7 W
Motor: P_out = rated (mechanical) power from the nameplate with η; otherwise η = 100 % (P = active input power).
```

**Inputs:** Solve for, System, Voltage U [V], Current I [A], Power P (output) [kW], Power factor cos φ, Efficiency η [%]

**Results:** Current I [A], Voltage U [V], Phase voltage U₀ (3~) [V], Output power P_out [kW], Output power [hp], Active input power P_in [kW], Apparent power S [kVA], Reactive power Q [kvar], Phase angle φ [°], Losses P_in − P_out [kW]

#### Power factor calculation

*cos φ from P and U·I, from P and S or from P and Q* — `power_factor_calc`

```text
cos φ = P / S    S = k · U · I (k = 1 for 1~, √3 for 3~)    S = √(P² + Q²)
Motor: P_in = P_out / η. φ = arccos(cos φ), Q = P · tan φ. With harmonics the power factor λ = P/S is smaller than cos φ₁.
```

**Inputs:** Known, System, Active power P [kW], Voltage U [V], Current I [A], Apparent power S [kVA], Reactive power Q [kvar], Efficiency η [%]

**Results:** Power factor cos φ, Phase angle φ [°], sin φ, tan φ, Apparent power S [kVA], Reactive power Q [kvar], Active input power P / η [kW]

#### Ohm's law & power

*U, I, R, P – enter any two values* — `ohm`

```text
Enter exactly two of the four quantities; the others are calculated.
U = R · I    P = U · I = I² · R = U² / R
```

**Inputs:** Voltage U [V], Current I [A], Resistance R [Ω], Power P [W]

**Results:** Voltage U [V], Current I [A], Resistance R [Ω], Power P [W]

#### Impedance calculation

*Z, R and X from voltage, current and cos φ* — `impedance_ui`

```text
1~: Z = U / I    3~ (per phase, star equivalent): Z = U / (√3 · I)
R = Z · cos φ    X = Z · sin φ
```

**Inputs:** System, Voltage U [V], Current I [A], Power factor cos φ

**Results:** Impedance Z [Ω], Resistance R [Ω], Reactance X [Ω], Phase angle φ [°]

#### Operating current

*I_b with utilisation and simultaneity factor* — `operating_current`

```text
P_b = P_inst · k_u · k_s    I_b = P_b / (k · U · cos φ · η)
k_u = utilisation factor (load / rated power, motors typ. 0.75), k_s = simultaneity factor of the group (e.g. 0.8–0.9 for a board with 4–9 circuits, IEC 61439-1 Tab. 101 as a guide).
```

**Inputs:** System, Voltage U [V], Installed power P_inst [kW], Utilisation factor k_u, Simultaneity factor k_s, Power factor cos φ, Efficiency η [%]

**Results:** Operating current I_b [A], Operating power P_b [kW], Current at full load (k_u = k_s = 1) [A]

#### Joule effect

*Joule heat W = I² · R · t and temperature rise* — `joule_effect`

```text
P = I² · R    W = I² · R · t    ΔT = W / (m · c) (no heat losses)
c: water 4186, copper 385, aluminium 897, air 1005 J/(kg·K).
```

**Inputs:** Resistance R [Ω], Current I [A], Time t [s], Mass m [kg], Specific heat capacity c [J/(kg·K)]

**Results:** Power P [W], Heat W [kJ], Heat W [Wh], Temperature rise ΔT [K]

#### Energy consumption & cost

*kWh and cost per day/year* — `energy_cost`

```text
E = P · t    Cost = E · price
Currency (€ or CHF) and exchange rate in the settings; the amount is also shown in the other currency.
```

**Inputs:** Power P [W], Operating hours per day [h], Operating days per year [d], Energy price per kWh [€ / CHF]

**Results:** Energy per day [kWh], Energy per year [kWh], Cost per day [€], Cost per year [€]

#### Resistors: series & parallel

*R_total for up to 6 resistors (also L)* — `resistors_series_parallel`

```text
Series: R = R1 + R2 + …
Parallel: 1/R = 1/R1 + 1/R2 + …
Also valid for uncoupled inductors. Empty fields are ignored.
```

**Inputs:** R1 [Ω], R2 [Ω], R3 [Ω], R4 [Ω], R5 [Ω], R6 [Ω]

**Results:** Series connection [Ω], Parallel connection [Ω]

#### Capacitors: series & parallel

*C_total for up to 6 capacitors* — `capacitors_series_parallel`

```text
Parallel: C = C1 + C2 + …
Series: 1/C = 1/C1 + 1/C2 + …
Empty fields are ignored.
```

**Inputs:** C1 [µF], C2 [µF], C3 [µF], C4 [µF], C5 [µF], C6 [µF]

**Results:** Series connection [µF], Parallel connection [µF]

#### Current divider

*Splitting a current across parallel resistors* — `current_divider`

```text
Parallel circuit: U = I · R_tot, 1/R_tot = Σ 1/R_i    I_i = U / R_i
Two resistors: I₁ = I · R₂ / (R₁ + R₂).
```

**Inputs:** Total current I [A], R₁ [Ω], R₂ [Ω], R₃ [Ω]

**Results:** Current through R₁ [A], Current through R₂ [A], Current through R₃ [A], Voltage U [V], Equivalent resistance R_tot [Ω]

#### Delta-star transformation

*Δ ↔ Y for resistors or impedances, with drawing* — `delta_star`

```text
Δ → Y: R₁ = R₁₂ · R₃₁ / ΣR    R₂ = R₁₂ · R₂₃ / ΣR    R₃ = R₂₃ · R₃₁ / ΣR    (ΣR = R₁₂ + R₂₃ + R₃₁)
Y → Δ: R₁₂ = (R₁R₂ + R₂R₃ + R₃R₁) / R₃    R₂₃ = … / R₁    R₃₁ = … / R₂
Balanced: R_Y = R_Δ / 3. Also valid for complex impedances.
```

**Inputs:** Transformation, R₁₂ (between 1 and 2) [Ω], R₂₃ (between 2 and 3) [Ω], R₃₁ (between 3 and 1) [Ω], R₁ (at node 1) [Ω], R₂ (at node 2) [Ω], R₃ (at node 3) [Ω]

**Results:** R₁ [Ω], R₂ [Ω], R₃ [Ω], R₁₂ [Ω], R₂₃ [Ω], R₃₁ [Ω]

### Cable calculations

#### Cable sizing

*Cross-section by ampacity, voltage drop and protective device* — `cable_sizing`

```text
The smallest copper cross-section meeting all conditions is selected:
1) I_z = I_z0 · f_T · f_G ≥ I_B    2) ΔU ≤ ΔU_max    3) I_B ≤ I_n ≤ I_z and I_2 ≤ 1.45 · I_z
f_T for ambient temperature (Tab. B.52.14/15), f_G grouping (Tab. B.52.17). Voltage drop at max. conductor temperature (70/90 °C), X' = 0.08 mΩ/m.
Copper current-carrying capacity per IEC 60364-5-52 Annex B (equivalent to DIN VDE 0298-4 / NIN 5.2.3), reference 30 °C air or 20 °C ground. Check values against the edition in force before use.
```

**Inputs:** System, Nominal voltage U [V], Design current I_B [A], Cable length L (one way) [m], Power factor cos φ, Permitted voltage drop ΔU max [%], Insulation, Installation method, Ambient temperature θ_a [°C], Grouping – arrangement, Number of grouped circuits, Protective device

**Results:** Temperature factor f_T, Grouping factor f_G, Required I_z0 [A], Min. area (ampacity) [mm²], Min. area (voltage drop) [mm²], Selected cross-section [mm²], Current capacity I_z [A], Protective device I_n [A], Voltage drop [%], Decisive

#### Voltage drop

*ΔU in V and %, losses, DE/CH limit* — `voltage_drop`

```text
ΔU = k · L · I · (ρ(θ)/A · cos φ + X' · sin φ)
k = 2 for DC/1~ (out and return), k = √3 for 3~ (line-to-line voltage)
ΔU % = ΔU / U · 100    P_loss = n · I² · ρ · L / A
Typically X' ≈ 0.08 mΩ/m for multi-core cables. 70 °C conductor temperature (PVC) is conservative; IEC 60364-5-52 Annex G uses ρ = 0.0225 Ω·mm²/m (Cu).
Limits: DIN 18015-1, DIN VDE 0100-520 Annex G, NIN – check against the edition in force and local rules.
```

**Inputs:** System, Nominal voltage U [V], Load current I_B [A], Cable length L (one way) [m], Cross-section A [mm²], Conductor material, Power factor cos φ, Conductor temperature θ [°C], Reactance per length X' [mΩ/m], Limit, Custom limit ΔU max [%]

**Results:** Voltage drop ΔU [V], Voltage drop ΔU [%], Voltage at load [V], Cable losses P_loss [W], Limit [%], Max. length at limit [m]

#### Max. cable length (voltage drop)

*e.g. 24 V field devices, actuators* — `max_length_vdrop`

```text
L_max = ΔU_max / (k · I · (ρ/A · cos φ + X' · sin φ))
k = 2 for DC/1~, √3 for 3~.
```

**Inputs:** System, Nominal voltage U [V], Current I [A], Cross-section A [mm²], Conductor material, Power factor cos φ, Conductor temperature θ [°C], Permitted voltage drop ΔU max [%]

**Results:** Max. cable length (one way) [m], Permitted voltage drop [V], Min. voltage at device [V]

#### Voltage drop with distributed loads

*n equal loads along a line (e.g. lighting row)* — `vdrop_distributed`

```text
Moment method: ΔU = k · Σ(I · L_i) · (ρ/A · cos φ + X' · sin φ), L_i = L₁ + (i − 1) · d
k = 2 (DC/1~), √3 (3~, balanced); ρ at 70 °C, X' = 0.08 mΩ/m. Σ(I·L) equals the total current at the equivalent length L₁ + (n − 1) · d / 2.
Cross-section: A = ρ · cos φ / (ΔU_max/(k · Σ I·L) − X' · sin φ), then the next standard size.
```

**Inputs:** System, Nominal voltage U [V], Number of loads n, Current per load [A], Distance to the first load L₁ [m], Spacing between loads d [m], Conductor material, Power factor cos φ, Cross-section

**Results:** Voltage drop at the last load [%], Voltage drop ΔU [V], Voltage at the last load [V], Total current at the start [A], Equivalent length [m], Length to the last load [m]

#### Cable sizing for distributed loads

*Minimum cross-section for n equal loads along a line* — `sizing_distributed`

```text
Moment method: ΔU = k · Σ(I · L_i) · (ρ/A · cos φ + X' · sin φ), L_i = L₁ + (i − 1) · d
k = 2 (DC/1~), √3 (3~, balanced); ρ at 70 °C, X' = 0.08 mΩ/m. Σ(I·L) equals the total current at the equivalent length L₁ + (n − 1) · d / 2.
Cross-section: A = ρ · cos φ / (ΔU_max/(k · Σ I·L) − X' · sin φ), then the next standard size.
```

**Inputs:** System, Nominal voltage U [V], Number of loads n, Current per load [A], Distance to the first load L₁ [m], Spacing between loads d [m], Conductor material, Power factor cos φ, Permissible voltage drop [%]

**Results:** Selected cross-section [mm²], Minimum cross-section (calculated) [mm²], Voltage drop with selected cross-section [%], Total current at the start [A], Equivalent length [m]

#### Current-carrying capacity I_z

*I_z by method, temperature and grouping* — `ampacity`

```text
I_z = I_z0 · f_T · f_G
f_T = √((θ_max − θ_a)/(θ_max − θ_ref))
Copper current-carrying capacity per IEC 60364-5-52 Annex B (equivalent to DIN VDE 0298-4 / NIN 5.2.3), reference 30 °C air or 20 °C ground. Check values against the edition in force before use.
```

**Inputs:** Cross-section (Cu), Insulation, Installation method, Loaded conductors, Ambient temperature θ_a [°C], Grouping – arrangement, Number of grouped circuits

**Results:** Table value I_z0 [A], Temperature factor f_T, Grouping factor f_G, Current capacity I_z [A]

#### Current rating of bare and mineral-insulated cables

*MI cables (Cu), sheath 70 °C, method C* — `ampacity_mi`

```text
Mineral-insulated copper cables (MICC) per IEC 60702-1, sheath PVC covered or bare exposed to touch (sheath temperature 70 °C), clipped direct (method C), 30 °C ambient. Values per BS 7671 Tab. 4G1A = IEC 60364-5-52 Tab. B.52.6 (manufacturer data sheet). 1~ = 2 loaded conductors, 3~ = 3 loaded conductors.
Ambient: f = √((70 − θ_a)/(70 − 30)) as an approximation of Tab. B.52.14 – for bare cables not exposed to touch (sheath 105 °C, Tab. B.52.7) and grouping use the standard. Check against the edition in force.
```

**Inputs:** Grade, Cores / loading, Cross-section, Ambient temperature θ_a [°C], Operating current I_b [A]

**Results:** Current rating I_z [A], Table value (30 °C) [A], Ambient temperature factor, Cable reference

#### Current rating of busbars

*E-Cu flat busbars per DIN 43671, 1 or 2 bars per conductor* — `busbar_ampacity`

```text
Continuous currents for E-Cu bars with rectangular cross-section, indoors, 25 °C air around the conductor and max. 65 °C bar temperature, AC, bars on edge (Striebel & John catalogue ch. 10, after DIN 43671).
Other ambient temperature as an approximation: I = I₂₅ · √((65 − θ_a)/(65 − 25)) (heat dissipation ∝ temperature rise). For design use the correction factors of DIN 43671 or manufacturer data (arrangement, paint, altitude, frequency).
```

**Inputs:** Bar W × T, Bars per conductor, Ambient temperature θ_a [°C], Operating current I_b [A]

**Results:** Continuous current at θ_a [A], Table value (25 °C) [A], Cross-section [mm²], Current density [A/mm²], Weight per conductor [kg/m]

#### Conductor resistance, reactance and impedance

*R(θ) = ρ(θ) · L / A, X and Z per conductor and loop, ρ and κ* — `cable_rxz`

```text
R₂₀ = ρ₂₀ · L / A    R(θ) = R₂₀ · (1 + α₂₀ · (θ − 20 °C))    X = X' · L    Z = √(R² + X²)
Cu: ρ₂₀ = 0.017241 Ω·mm²/m, α = 0.00393 1/K; Al: 0.028264 Ω·mm²/m, 0.00403 1/K. X' ≈ 0.08 mΩ/m (multi-core cables), approx. 0.1–0.15 mΩ/m for spaced single cores. Loop 1~/DC = go and return conductor; 3~ = per phase.
DC resistance without skin effect (noticeable at 50 Hz from approx. 150 mm²).
```

**Inputs:** System, Conductor material, Cross-section, Length L (one way) [m], Conductor temperature θ [°C], Reactance per length X' [mΩ/m]

**Results:** R₂₀ per conductor [mΩ], R(θ) per conductor [mΩ], X per conductor [mΩ], Z per conductor [mΩ], Z loop (go + return) [mΩ], R(θ) per km [Ω/km], R(θ) loop (go + return) [mΩ], Resistivity ρ(θ) [Ω·mm²/m], Conductivity κ(θ) [m/(Ω·mm²)]

#### Cable temperature

*Conductor temperature from loading I_b/I_z* — `cable_temperature`

```text
θ = θ_a + (θ_z − θ_a) · (I_b / I_z)²
θ_z = permissible conductor temperature (PVC 70 °C, XLPE/EPR 90 °C), I_z = current rating at the actual ambient θ_a and grouping. Losses ∝ I² (IEC 60287, ignoring the change of R with temperature).
```

**Inputs:** Operating current I_b [A], Current rating I_z [A], Ambient temperature θ_a [°C], Insulation

**Results:** Conductor temperature θ [°C], Temperature rise [K], Loading I_b/I_z [%], R(θ)/R₂₀ (Cu)

#### Power loss in cables

*P_loss = n · I² · R(θ), energy and cost per year* — `cable_losses`

```text
P_loss = n · I² · ρ(θ) · L / A    n = 2 (DC/1~), 3 (3~, balanced)
E = P_loss · operating hours    Cost = E · energy price. θ = mean conductor temperature (see cable temperature), neutral without current.
```

**Inputs:** System, Current I [A], Length L (one way) [m], Conductor material, Cross-section, Conductor temperature θ [°C], Voltage U (for %) [V], Power factor cos φ, Operating hours per year [h], Energy price per kWh [€ / CHF]

**Results:** Power loss P_loss [W], Loss per metre of cable [W/m], Share of the transmitted power [%], Energy loss per year [kWh], Loss cost per year [€]

#### Neutral current

*Unbalance and 3rd harmonic, sizing per IEC 60364-5-52 Annex E* — `neutral_current`

```text
Fundamental (same cos φ, 120° apart): I_N1 = √(I₁² + I₂² + I₃² − I₁I₂ − I₂I₃ − I₃I₁)
3rd harmonic (h₃ in % of the line current) adds up in N: I_N3 = h₃ · (I₁ + I₂ + I₃)    I_N = √(I_N1² + I_N3²)
Sizing (IEC 60364-5-52 Tab. E.52.1, 4-/5-core cables): h₃ ≤ 15 %: line current, factor 1.0; 15–33 %: line current, 0.86; 33–45 %: neutral current, 0.86; > 45 %: neutral current, 1.0.
```

**Inputs:** Current L1 [A], Current L2 [A], Current L3 [A], 3rd harmonic content h₃ [%]

**Results:** Neutral current I_N [A], Unbalance part I_N1 [A], 3rd harmonic part I_N3 [A], I_N / highest line current [%], Design current for cable selection [A], Sizing

#### Short-circuit withstand (I²t)

*Permissible energy and time of a cable* — `short_circuit_withstand`

```text
Adiabatic heating (IEC 60364-4-43, 434.5.2): t = (k · S / I_k)²   or   S ≥ √(I_k² · t) / k
Permissible energy: (k · S)²   k per Tab. 43A: Cu/PVC 115, Cu/XLPE 143, Al/PVC 76, Al/XLPE 94.
Valid for t ≤ 5 s. For t < 0.1 s compare with the device's let-through I²t (manufacturer data).
```

**Inputs:** Conductor / insulation, Cross-section S [mm²], Short-circuit current I_k [kA], Disconnection time t [s]

**Results:** Material factor k [A·√s/mm²], Permissible energy (k·S)² [A²s], Max. disconnection time at I_k [s], Min. cross-section for I_k, t [mm²]

#### Conduit fill

*Fill ratio of a conduit* — `conduit_fill`

```text
Fill = n · d² / D_i²  (cross-sectional areas)
Guide values (e.g. NEC Chapter 9): 1 cable 53 %, 2 cables 31 %, 3 or more 40 %. Cables must be installable without damage – follow manufacturer data.
```

**Inputs:** Conduit inner diameter D_i [mm], Cable outer diameter d [mm], Number of cables

**Results:** Fill ratio [%], Guide value [%], Max. number at 40 %

### Cable tables & colours

#### Voltage drop limits DE/CH

| Country | Rule | Section | ΔU max |
|---|---|---|---|
| DE | DIN 18015-1 | meter to load (residential) | 3 % |
| DE | VDE-AR-N 4100 (TAB) | service to meter | 0.5 % (≤ 100 kVA) |
| DE | DIN VDE 0100-520 Annex G | public LV – lighting / other | 3 % / 5 % |
| DE | DIN VDE 0100-520 Annex G | private supply – lighting / other | 6 % / 8 % |
| CH | NIN | service fuse to load | 4 % |

*Overview – the editions in force and local utility rules are authoritative.*

| mm² | A1 | A2 | B1 | B2 | C | D1 | E |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1.5 | 14.5 | 14 | 17.5 | 16.5 | 19.5 | 22 | 22 |
| 2.5 | 19.5 | 18.5 | 24 | 23 | 27 | 29 | 30 |
| 4 | 26 | 25 | 32 | 30 | 36 | 37 | 40 |
| 6 | 34 | 32 | 41 | 38 | 46 | 46 | 51 |

#### Conductor colours (Europe and worldwide)

| Country / system | L1 | L2 | L3 | N | PE |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Europe (IEC 60445) | brown | black | grey | blue | green-yellow |
| UK before 2004 | red | yellow | blue | black | green-yellow |
| USA 120/208 V | black | red | blue | white | green |
| USA 277/480 V | brown | orange | yellow | grey | green |
| Canada | red | black | blue | white | green |
| Australia / New Zealand | red | white | dark blue | black | green-yellow |
| China | yellow | green | red | light blue | green-yellow |

*Europe: IEC 60445 / HD 308 S2 (since 2004 in DE, UK after 2004). PEN conductor: green-yellow with blue marking at the ends (or blue with green-yellow marking). Machines per IEC 60204-1 clause 13.2 (recommendation): AC/DC power circuits black, AC control circuits red, DC control circuits blue, external supply (live with main switch OFF) orange. USA/Canada: NEC/CEC only mandate N (white/grey) and ground (green, green-yellow or bare), line colours are customary. Without guarantee – always measure before work.*

### Protection & fuses

#### Overload protection (coordination)

*I_B ≤ I_n ≤ I_z and I_2 ≤ 1.45 · I_z* — `overload_protection`

```text
Conditions per IEC 60364-4-43:
(1) I_B ≤ I_n ≤ I_z    (2) I_2 ≤ 1.45 · I_z
I_2 = 1.45 · I_n for MCBs (IEC 60898-1); gG: 2.1 (≤ 4 A), 1.9 (< 16 A), 1.6 (≥ 16 A) · I_n (IEC 60269-1).
```

**Inputs:** Design current I_B [A], Current capacity I_z [A], Protective device

**Results:** Rated current I_n [A], Conventional tripping current I_2 [A], Limit 1.45 · I_z [A]

#### Disconnection / max. length (TN)

*Z_s,max and L_max for MCB B/C/D* — `disconnection`

```text
Disconnection in TN systems (IEC 60364-4-41, 411.4.4): Z_s · I_a ≤ U_0
Conventional method: L_max = 0.8 · U_0 · S_ph / (ρ · (1 + m) · I_a), m = S_ph / S_PE
0.8: 80 % of U_0 at the origin of the circuit; ρ = 0.0225 (Cu) or 0.036 (Al) Ω·mm²/m.
I_a = instantaneous trip of MCB: B 5 · I_n, C 10 · I_n, D 20 · I_n. For fuses take I_a from the curve.
Does not replace measuring the loop impedance.
```

**Inputs:** Voltage to earth U_0 [V], Protective device, Rated current I_n [A], Disconnection current I_a [A], Phase cross-section S_ph [mm²], PE cross-section S_PE [mm²], Conductor material, Planned length [m]

**Results:** Disconnection current I_a [A], Max. loop impedance Z_s [Ω], Max. cable length L_max [m]

#### Tripping curves

*Time-current zone of MCBs B, C, D (IEC 60898-1)* — `trip_curves`

```text
Tripping zone between no-trip and trip curve from the IEC 60898-1 test points: 1.13 · I_n ≥ 1 h, 1.45 · I_n < 1 h, 2.55 · I_n 1 … 60 s (I_n ≤ 32 A), magnetic B 3–5, C 5–10, D 10–20 · I_n (< 0.1 s). Between the test points schematic (straight in the log-log chart). Exact curves from the manufacturer.
With a fault current: tripping time zone at the fault (disconnection: trip in the magnetic range).
```

**Inputs:** Characteristic, Rated current I_n [A], Fault current [A]

**Results:** Magnetic trip guaranteed from [A], No instantaneous trip up to [A], I_k / I_n, Zone at I_k

#### Tripping curves: test currents

| Device | No tripping | Tripping | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| MCB B, C, D (thermal) | 1.13 · I_n ≥ 1 h | 1.45 · I_n < 1 h | 2.55 · I_n: 1–60 s (I_n ≤ 32 A), 1–120 s (> 32 A) |
| MCB B (magnetic) | 3 · I_n ≥ 0.1 s | 5 · I_n < 0.1 s | cable protection |
| MCB C (magnetic) | 5 · I_n ≥ 0.1 s | 10 · I_n < 0.1 s | motors, luminaires |
| MCB D (magnetic) | 10 · I_n ≥ 0.1 s | 20 · I_n < 0.1 s | high inrush |
| MCB K | 1.05 · I_n; 8 · I_n | 1.2 · I_n; 14 · I_n | IEC 60947-2, manufacturer data |
| MCB Z | 1.05 · I_n; 2 · I_n | 1.2 · I_n; 3 · I_n | semiconductors, measuring circuits |
| Fuse gG (I_n ≥ 16 A) | 1.25 · I_n (I_nf) | 1.6 · I_n (I_f) | test time 1–4 h depending on I_n |
| RCD general type | 0.5 · I_Δn | I_Δn ≤ 300 ms, 2 · I_Δn ≤ 150 ms, 5 · I_Δn ≤ 40 ms | type AC, A, F, B by residual current waveform |
| RCD selective (S) | I_Δn: ≥ 130 ms | I_Δn ≤ 500 ms, 5 · I_Δn ≤ 150 ms | upstream for selectivity |

*MCBs IEC 60898-1 (B, C, D) or IEC 60947-2 (K, Z); gG fuses IEC 60269-2; RCDs IEC 61008/61009 (break times at rated residual current, general/selective S). Curves: calculator “Tripping curves”. Check against the edition in force.*

#### Fuses: utilisation categories

| Class | Range | Protected object / use |
|---|---|---|
| gG | full range | cables, general purpose |
| gL | full range | old German marking for cable protection, now gG |
| aM | partial range | motor circuits (short circuit, overload by relay) |
| gR | full range | semiconductors (very fast) |
| gS | full range | semiconductors and cables |
| aR | partial range | semiconductors (short circuit) |
| gTr | full range | transformers (LV side) |
| gPV | full range | photovoltaic strings (DC, IEC 60269-6) |

*IEC 60269-1/-2/-4/-6. 1st letter: g = full range (overload and short circuit), a = partial range (short circuit only, overload protection by other devices). 2nd letter: protected object.*

#### D-type fuses (Neozed/Diazed): sizes and indicator colours

| I_n | Indicator | Neozed (D0) | Diazed (D) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2 A | pink | D01 | DII |
| 4 A | brown | D01 | DII |
| 6 A | green | D01 | DII |
| 10 A | red | D01 | DII |
| 13 A | black | D01 | DII |
| 16 A | grey | D01 | DII |
| 20 A | blue | D02 | DII |
| 25 A | yellow | D02 | DII |
| 35 A | black | D02 | DIII |
| 50 A | white | D02 | DIII |
| 63 A | copper | D02 | DIII |
| 80 A | silver | D03 | DIV |
| 100 A | red | D03 | DIV |

*IEC 60269-3 (D system, usable by ordinary persons). Neozed D0: D01 E14 (2–16 A), D02 E18 (20–63 A), D03 M30 × 2 (80–100 A), 400 V AC. Diazed D: DII E27 (2–25 A), DIII E33 (35–63 A), DIV R 1¼" (80–100 A), 500 V AC. Gauge screws/rings prevent oversized cartridges. Size = range of the size; individual ratings depend on the manufacturer.*

#### NH fuses: sizes

|---|---|---|---|

| NH00 | up to 160 A | 500/690 V AC | common in distribution boards |
| NH0 | up to 160 A | 500/690 V AC | not for new installations |

| NH2 | up to 400 A | 500/690 V AC | – |
| NH3 | up to 630 A | 500/690 V AC | – |

*IEC 60269-2 (NH blade fuses, to be operated by skilled or instructed persons only, with fuse puller and sleeve). Highest rated currents per size for 500/690 V AC; check manufacturer data.*

#### Cylindrical fuses

| Size | Rated current up to | Voltage | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| 8,5 × 31,5 mm | 20 A | 400 V AC | small loads |
| 10 × 38 mm | 32 A | 400–690 V AC | also gPV up to 1000/1500 V DC |
| 14 × 51 mm | 50 A | 400–690 V AC | also aM for motors |
| 22 × 58 mm | 125 A | 400–690 V AC | also aM for motors |

*IEC 60269-2 (cylindrical fuse links, e.g. for fuse disconnectors). Highest usual rated currents; smaller depending on manufacturer and voltage – check the data sheet.*

#### Miniature fuses (G fuses)

| Symbol | Characteristic | Use |
|---|---|---|
| FF | very fast acting | semiconductors, meters |
| F | fast acting | loads without inrush |
| M | medium time lag | small inrush |
| T | time lag | power supplies, transformers, motors |
| TT | long time lag | high inrush |

*IEC 60127. Sizes 5 × 20 mm and 6.3 × 32 mm. Breaking capacity: L = low (glass tube, typ. 35 A or 10 · I_N), E = enhanced, H = high (ceramic with sand filling, 1500 A). Marking e.g. T 1.6 A H 250 V.*

#### Automotive blade fuses: colours

| Current | Colour |
|---|---|
| 1 A | black |
| 2 A | grey |
| 3 A | violet |
| 4 A | pink |
| 5 A | tan |
| 7,5 A | brown |
| 10 A | red |
| 15 A | blue |
| 20 A | yellow |
| 25 A | clear/white |
| 30 A | green |

*ISO 8820-3 (blade fuses ATO/ATC and mini). 35 A is not common in all designs. Maxi fuses partly use other colours.*

#### UL/CSA fuse classes

| Class | Voltage | Ampere rating | Interrupting rating | Feature / use |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CC | 600 V AC | 0–30 A | 200 kA | small (13/32" × 1½"), control circuits, motors |
| CD | 600 V AC | 35–60 A | 200 kA | like CC, higher currents |
| G | 480–600 V AC | 0–60 A | 100 kA | length differs per current range |
| H | 250/600 V AC | 0–600 A | 10 kA | older design, low interrupting rating |
| J | 600 V AC | 0–600 A | 200 kA | current-limiting, compact |
| K | 250/600 V AC | 0–600 A | 50–200 kA | dimensions as H, no rejection |
| L | 600 V AC | 601–6000 A | 200 kA | bolt-in, main feeders |
| R (RK1, RK5) | 250/600 V AC | 0–600 A | 200 kA | rejection groove, motor circuits |
| T | 300/600 V AC | 0–1200 A | 200 kA | very compact, very fast |

*UL 248 / CSA C22.2 No. 248. Rejection classes (CC, J, L, R, T) do not accept fuses with lower interrupting rating. Overview values – check manufacturer data and the edition in force.*

### Short circuit & earthing

#### Short-circuit current at cable end

*I_k3 max and I_k1 min (IEC 60909 estimate)* — `short_circuit`

```text
Simplified estimate per IEC 60909-0 (without correction factor K_T):
I_k3 = c_max · U_n / (√3 · |Z_Q + Z_T + Z_L|),  c_max = 1.05, conductors at 20 °C
I_k1 = c_min · U_0 / |⅔Z_Q + Z_T + Z_L + Z_PE|,  c_min = 0.95, conductors at 80 °C (Dyn transformer)
Z_Q = c · U_n² / S"_kQ (X/R = 10), Z_T = u_k · U_n² / S_rT, R_T = u_R · U_n² / S_rT.
For binding design use network calculation software or utility data.
```

**Inputs:** Transformer rating S_rT [kVA], Short-circuit voltage u_k [%], Resistive part u_R [%], Nominal voltage U_n [V], Network fault level S"_kQ (empty = infinite) [MVA], Cable length L [m], Phase cross-section [mm²], PE/PEN cross-section [mm²], Conductor material, Reactance per length X' [mΩ/m]

**Results:** I_k3 at transformer terminals [kA], I_k3 max at cable end [kA], I_k1 min at cable end [kA], Transformer impedance Z_T [mΩ], Cable impedance Z_L (20 °C) [mΩ]

#### Minimum short-circuit current

*I_k1min, I_k2min at the end of a cable (c_min = 0.95)* — `short_circuit_min`

```text
I_k1min = c_min · U₀ / |Z_up + Z_L + Z_PE|    I_k2min = c_min · √3 · U₀ / |Z_up + 2 · Z_L|
c_min = 0.95 (LV, IEC 60909-0). Conductor resistance at end temperature θ (end of short circuit, usually 80 °C for PVC, or operating temperature 70/90 °C). Z_up = loop impedance at the origin of the cable (L–PE, measured or from the utility), X'_L = 0.08 mΩ/m per conductor.
Check: I_k1min ≥ I_a of the protective device (disconnection).
```

**Inputs:** Nominal voltage to earth U₀ [V], Loop impedance at the origin Z_up [mΩ], Conductor material, Cable length L [m], Line conductor cross-section, PE cross-section, Conductor temperature θ [°C], Tripping current I_a of the device [A]

**Results:** I_k1min (L–PE) [A], I_k1min (L–N, N as PE) [A], I_k2min (L–L) [A], Loop impedance Z_s at the end [mΩ], I_k1min / I_a

#### Earthing systems TN, TT, IT (diagram)

*Layout of TN-S, TN-C, TN-C-S, TT and IT with drawing* — `earthing_systems`

```text
IEC 60364-1: 1st letter earthing of the source (T = directly earthed, I = isolated or high impedance), 2nd letter earthing of exposed parts (T = own earth electrode, N = via the protective conductor to the star point). S = N and PE separate, C = combined as PEN. Protection see calculators earthing/RCD and disconnection.
```

**Inputs:** System

**Results:** Source, Exposed parts, Fault protection, Note

#### Earthing system and coordination with RCD

*TT: R_A · I_Δn ≤ U_L    TN: Z_s · I_a ≤ U₀* — `earthing_rcd`

```text
TT system (IEC 60364-4-41, 411.5.3): R_A · I_Δn ≤ U_L, U_L = 50 V AC (25 V in special locations, e.g. agricultural premises). R_A = sum of earth electrode and protective conductor resistance.
TN system (411.4.4): Z_s · I_a ≤ U₀; with an RCD I_a = I_Δn (5 · I_Δn for 0.04 s).
Check against the edition in force.
```

**Inputs:** Earthing system, Rated residual current I_Δn, Touch voltage U_L, Nominal voltage U₀ [V], Measured: R_A or Z_s [Ω]

**Results:** Permissible resistance (R_A or Z_s) [Ω], Touch voltage at I_Δn [V], Max. I_Δn for the measured resistance [mA]

### Transformer & power factor correction

#### Transformer: currents & I_k

*Rated currents, ratio, short-circuit current* — `transformer`

```text
I = S / (√3 · U) for 3~ or S / U for 1~    ratio = U1 / U2
I_k ≈ c · I_r2 · 100 % / u_k   (infinite network, c = 1.05);  steady short-circuit current I_kD = I_r · 100 % / u_k (no c)
Typical u_k (Haag vol. 05 transformers, 2007): distribution transformers 10 … 20 kV 4 … 6 %, 110 kV 10 … 14 %, 380 kV 11 … 20 %. Haag example: 400 kVA, u_k = 4 % → I_r = 577 A, I_kD = 14.43 kA. “Stiff” transformers (small u_k) give higher short-circuit currents.
```

**Inputs:** Type, Rated power S_r [kVA], Primary voltage U_1 [V], Secondary voltage U_2 [V], Short-circuit voltage u_k [%]

**Results:** Rated primary current [A], Rated secondary current [A], Ratio, Secondary short-circuit current I_k [kA], Steady short-circuit current I_kD (no c) [kA]

#### Primary/secondary windings of transformers

*Ratio, currents and number of turns (small transformer)* — `transformer_windings`

```text
ratio = U₁/U₂ = N₁/N₂ = I₂/I₁    Turns per volt w = 1 / (4.44 · f · B · A_core)
N₁ = w · U₁    N₂ = w · U₂ · (1 + allowance) (compensates voltage drops, small transformers 5–10 %)
B: electrical steel 1.0–1.4 T (EI laminations). If not given A_core ≈ √S cm² (rule of thumb for EI cores).
```

**Inputs:** Rated power S [VA], Primary voltage U₁ [V], Secondary voltage U₂ [V], Frequency, Flux density B [T], Core cross-section A_core [cm²], Allowance secondary turns [%]

**Results:** Turns ratio, Primary current I₁ [A], Secondary current I₂ [A], Turns per volt [1/V], Primary turns N₁, Secondary turns N₂, Core cross-section used [cm²]

#### Power factor correction

*Q_C and capacitor from cos φ1 to cos φ2* — `pf_correction`

```text
Q_C = P · (tan φ1 − tan φ2)
Capacitance per capacitor (U = line voltage, ω = 2πf):
Delta: C = Q_C / (3 · U² · ω)    Star: C = Q_C / (U² · ω)    1~: C = Q_C / (U² · ω)
Use detuned banks where harmonics are present.
```

**Inputs:** Active power P [kW], cos φ1 (actual), cos φ2 (target), Voltage U [V], Frequency f [Hz], Connection

**Results:** Compensation power Q_C [kvar], Capacitance per capacitor [µF], Apparent power before [kVA], Apparent power after [kVA], Current before [A], Current after [A]

#### Reactive power compensation of MV/LV transformers

*No-load and load reactive power, fixed compensation* — `transformer_pfc`

```text
No-load: Q₀ = i₀ · S_N (magnetising, always present)
Load: Q_L = u_k · S_N · (S/S_N)² (leakage field)
Fixed compensation of the transformer ≈ Q₀ (capacitor on the LV side, permanently connected). i₀ and u_k from the rating plate or test report; oil transformers typ. i₀ = 0.5–2 %, cast resin 0.5–1.5 %. Check the maximum fixed compensation with the utility's connection rules (resonance, over-compensation).
```

**Inputs:** Rated power S_N [kVA], No-load current i₀ [%], Short-circuit voltage u_k [%], Load S [kVA]

**Results:** No-load reactive power Q₀ [kvar], Load reactive power Q_L [kvar], Total Q₀ + Q_L [kvar], Loading S/S_N [%]

#### Capacitor power at different voltages

*Q = Q_N · (U/U_N)² · (f/f_N) and capacitance* — `capacitor_voltage`

```text
Q = Q_N · (U/U_N)² · (f/f_N)
Capacitance (3~): delta C_Δ = Q / (3 · 2πf · U²) per capacitor, star C_Y = Q / (2πf · U²) per capacitor (U = line voltage). 1~: C = Q / (2πf · U²).
Capacitors may be operated continuously at no more than 1.1 · U_N (IEC 60831-1).
```

**Inputs:** Rated power Q_N [kvar], Rated voltage U_N [V], Rated frequency f_N, Operating voltage U [V], System frequency f, Design

**Results:** Power at U and f [kvar], Q / Q_N [%], Capacitance per capacitor [µF], Current (line) [A]

### Enclosures, cabinets & safety

#### IP codes (ingress protection)

| Digit | 1st digit: solid objects | 1st digit: contact | 2nd digit: water |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | no protection | no protection | no protection |
| 1 | ≥ 50 mm | back of hand | vertically dripping water |
| 2 | ≥ 12.5 mm | finger | dripping water, tilted up to 15° |
| 3 | ≥ 2.5 mm | tool | spraying water up to 60° from vertical |
| 4 | ≥ 1.0 mm | wire | splashing water from any direction |
| 5 | dust-protected | wire | water jets (6.3 mm nozzle) |
| 6 | dust-tight | wire | powerful water jets (12.5 mm nozzle) |
| 7 | – | – | temporary immersion (1 m, 30 min) |
| 8 | – | – | continuous immersion (as agreed) |
| 9 | – | – | high-pressure, high-temperature jets (80 °C), IPX9 |

*IEC 60529 incl. amendment 2 (2013) for IPX9. Example IP54: dust-protected, wire; splashing water. X = not tested. Additional letters A–D (contact) and H, M, S, W possible.*

#### IK code (impact protection)

| IK | Energy | Mass | Drop height |
|---|---|---|---|
| IK00 | no protection | – | – |
| IK01 | 0,14 J | 0,25 kg | 56 mm |
| IK02 | 0,2 J | 0,25 kg | 80 mm |
| IK03 | 0,35 J | 0,25 kg | 140 mm |
| IK04 | 0,5 J | 0,25 kg | 200 mm |
| IK05 | 0,7 J | 0,25 kg | 280 mm |
| IK06 | 1 J | 0,25 kg | 400 mm |
| IK07 | 2 J | 0,5 kg | 400 mm |
| IK08 | 5 J | 1,7 kg | 300 mm |
| IK09 | 10 J | 5 kg | 200 mm |
| IK10 | 20 J | 5 kg | 400 mm |

*IEC 62262 / EN 62262: protection of enclosures against mechanical impacts. Test with a falling hammer (mass, height).*

#### NEMA enclosure types

| Type | Use / protection | ≈ IP |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | indoor, contact, falling dirt | IP10 |
| 2 | indoor, dripping water | IP11 |
| 3 | outdoor, rain, sleet, windblown dust, ice | IP54 |
| 3R | outdoor, rain, sleet, ice (not dust) | IP14 |
| 3S | as 3, operable when ice-laden | IP54 |
| 4 | indoor/outdoor, hose-directed water | IP56 |
| 4X | as 4 + corrosion resistant | IP56 |
| 5 | indoor, dust, dripping water | IP52 |
| 6 | temporary submersion | IP67 |
| 6P | prolonged submersion | IP67 |
| 7, 8, 9, 10 | hazardous locations / mining | – |
| 12, 12K | indoor industrial: dust, dripping oil, coolant | IP52 |
| 13 | indoor, dust, spraying oil and coolant | IP54 |

*NEMA 250 (North America). The IP code is the minimum equivalent per the NEMA conversion table – NEMA types require additional tests (corrosion, ice, oil); converting IP to NEMA is not permitted.*

#### Protection classes

| Class | Protective measure | Marking / note |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | basic insulation only | not permitted in DE/CH |
| I | basic insulation + PE connection | PE symbol ⏚ |
| II | double or reinforced insulation | double square ⧈, no PE |
| III | extra-low voltage SELV/PELV | ≤ 50 V AC / 120 V DC |

*IEC 61140*

#### Enclosure heat balance

*Internal temperature, cooling, fan* — `enclosure_cooling`

```text
Effective surface per IEC/TR 60890:
free-standing: A = 1.8 · H · (W + D) + 1.4 · W · D    wall-mounted: A = 1.4 · W · (H + D) + 1.8 · D · H
ΔT = P_loss / (k · A)    Cooling demand Q_c = P_loss − k · A · (θ_i,max − θ_a)
Fan: V̇ = 3.1 · Q / ΔT  [m³/h] (sea level). k ≈ 5.5 W/(m²·K) for sheet steel.
```

**Inputs:** Heat loss P_loss [W], Height H [m], Width W [m], Depth D [m], Mounting, Heat transfer coefficient k [W/(m²·K)], Ambient temperature θ_a [°C], Max. internal temperature θ_i [°C]

**Results:** Effective surface A [m²], Temperature rise without cooling ΔT [K], Internal temperature without cooling [°C], Required cooling capacity [W], Fan airflow (if θ_a < θ_i) [m³/h]

#### 5 safety rules & safety distances

| Topic | Value / rule |
|---|---|
| Rule 1 | Disconnect completely |
| Rule 2 | Secure against reconnection |
| Rule 3 | Verify absence of voltage (all poles) |
| Rule 4 | Earth and short-circuit |
| Rule 5 | Cover or fence off adjacent live parts |
| Safety distance skilled person ≤ 1 kV | 0,5 m |
| Safety distance skilled person 1 … 30 kV | 1,5 m |
| Safety distance skilled person 30 … 110 kV | 2,0 m |
| Safety distance skilled person 110 … 220 kV | 3,0 m |
| Safety distance skilled person 220 … 380 kV | 4,0 m |
| Construction/lay persons ≤ 1 kV | 1,0 m |
| Construction/lay persons 1 … 110 kV | 3,0 m |
| Construction/lay persons 110 … 220 kV | 4,0 m |
| Construction/lay persons 220 … 380 kV | 5,0 m |
| Danger zone ≤ 1 kV | Cover (no contact) |
| Danger zone 10 kV indoor / 20 kV / 110 kV | 120 / 220 / 1100 mm |

*Source: Haag “Grundlagen der Stromversorgung”, vol. 09 occupational safety (2007), p. 7–9 (DIN VDE 0105-100 tab. 101–103, extract). Check against DIN VDE 0105-100 and DGUV regulation 3 in force.*

```text
Symbols per IEC 60617 (circuit diagrams) and installation symbols per IEC 60617-11 (installation plans), simplified. Reference letters per IEC 81346-2 (e.g. R resistor, C capacitor, Q switching device, K relay, F protective device, M motor, P meter, E luminaire).
```

**Inputs:** Group

**Results:** Symbols

*IEEE C37.2 (ANSI): numbers for protection and switching functions, e.g. 50/51 for overcurrent protection. Suffixes: N = neutral/ground (residual), G = ground (measured), P = phase, V = voltage restrained (51V), T = transformer (87T).*

| Acronym | German | English |

| MCB | Leitungsschutzschalter (LS) | miniature circuit breaker |

| CT / VT | Strom-/Spannungswandler | current / voltage transformer |
| PLC | speicherprogrammierbare Steuerung (SPS) | programmable logic controller |
| HMI | Bedien- und Beobachtungsgerät | human-machine interface |
| SELV / PELV | Schutzkleinspannung / Funktionskleinspannung mit Erdung | safety / protective extra-low voltage |
| MV / LV | Mittelspannung / Niederspannung (MS/NS) | medium / low voltage |
| EMC | elektromagnetische Verträglichkeit (EMV) | electromagnetic compatibility |
| LOTO | Freischalten und Sichern (Lockout-Tagout) | lockout-tagout |

*Common acronyms in data sheets and standards (IEC/IEV) with the German name.*

## Motors & drives

### Motor feeder

#### Motor feeder: motor, pump, fan

*What the feeder needs: MPCB/MCB, contactors, soft starter/drive, cable, isolator, RCD* — `motor_feeder`

```text
Motor current: guide values for 4-pole squirrel-cage motors (Eaton wiring manual 06/11, p. 10-43/44) or nameplate.
Starting current: DOL ≤ 6 · I_N (t_A ≤ 5 s), star-delta ≤ 2 · I_N (t_A ≤ 15 s), soft start approx. 2–6 · I_N, drive ≈ 1 (…2) · I_N.
Overload I_r = I_N; star-delta: relay in the motor line 0.58 · I_N (t_A ≤ 15 s), in the supply I_N (15–40 s), above that in the delta connection (no protection while starting). Y-Δ contactors: line and delta 0.58 · I_N, star 0.33 · I_N (Eaton p. 8-37).
Trip class IEC 60947-4-1 at 7.2 · I_r: CLASS 10 > 4 s, 20 > 6 s, 30 > 9 s – the run-up time must stay below.
Motor protective circuit-breaker: setting ranges Eaton PKZM0 (0.1–32 A, short-circuit proof up to 50 kA at 400 V) and PKZM4 (up to 65 A).
Cable Cu/PVC with I_z ≥ I_r (IEC 60364-5-52, ambient and grouping factor); for drives/EC sized for the MCB I_n.
RCD for drives with B6 rectifier: type B (all-current sensitive) since smooth DC fault currents are possible (EN 50178, IEC 60364-5-53). Manufacturer coordination tables (type 1/2) take precedence.
```

**Inputs:** Application, Starting method, Direction, Protection concept, Motor power P_N [kW], Supply, Motor data, Rated current I_N (nameplate) [A], Power factor cos φ (nameplate), Run-up time t_A [s], Cable length (one way) [m], Installation method, Grouped circuits, Ambient temperature [°C]

**Results:** Rated motor current I_N [A], Starting current (approx.) [A], Overload setting I_r [A], Short-circuit and overload protection, Smallest gL/gG fuse (guide value) [A], Circuit-breaker I_n [A], Contactors (AC-3), Starter / drive, Conductor cross-section Cu [mm²], Current rating I_z [A], Voltage drop at I_N [%], Cable, Local isolator, Residual current device (RCD), Trip class / arrangement, Accessories and notes

### Motor data

#### Efficiency from measurement

*η from U, I, cos φ and output power* — `motor_efficiency`

```text
P_el = k · U · I · cos φ    η = P_mech / P_el    P_loss = P_el − P_mech
```

**Inputs:** System, Voltage U [V], Current I [A], Power factor cos φ, Output power P_mech [kW]

**Results:** Input power P_el [kW], Efficiency η [%], Losses P_loss [kW]

#### Torque · power · speed

*M = 9549 · P / n – enter any two* — `torque`

```text
M [Nm] = 9549.3 · P [kW] / n [1/min]    (P = 2π · n · M / 60)
Enter exactly two values.
```

**Inputs:** Power P [kW], Speed n [1/min], Torque M [Nm]

**Results:** Power P [kW], Speed n [1/min], Torque M [Nm]

#### Speed & slip

*Synchronous speed, slip, rotor frequency* — `motor_speed`

```text
n_s = 120 · f / poles = 60 · f / p    s = (n_s − n) / n_s    f_2 = s · f
```

**Inputs:** Frequency f [Hz], Number of poles, Rated speed n [1/min]

**Results:** Synchronous speed n_s [1/min], Slip s [%], Rotor frequency f_2 [Hz]

#### Pump/fan on VFD: affinity laws

*V̇ ~ n, Δp ~ n², P ~ n³ – power and saving with speed control* — `fan_affinity`

```text
V̇₂/V̇₁ = n₂/n₁ · Δp₂/Δp₁ = (n₂/n₁)² · P₂/P₁ = (n₂/n₁)³
Valid for quadratic system curves with constant density and efficiency (fans, closed circuits). Not applicable with a static part (head, inlet pressure).
Pumps and fans usually run at 50 … 90 % speed.
Sources: Siemens “Einführung in die HLK- und Gebäudetechnik” (08/2022), p. 180 (example 4000 → 8000 m³/h, 350 → 1400 Pa); Danfoss “Facts worth knowing about frequency converters”, ch. 3.
```

**Inputs:** Flow V̇₁ [m³/h], Pressure rise Δp₁ [Pa], Power input P₁ [kW], Speed n₁ [1/min], New operating point from, Speed n₂ [1/min], Flow V̇₂ [m³/h], Operating hours at new point [h/a], Electricity price [€ / CHF]

**Results:** Speed ratio n₂/n₁ [%], Speed n₂ [1/min], Flow V̇₂ [m³/h], Pressure rise Δp₂ [Pa], Power input P₂ [kW], Energy saving [kWh/a], Cost saving [€]

#### Motor full-load current (NEC)

*Table current per NEC 430.248 / 430.250 (North America)* — `nec_flc`

```text
NEC (NFPA 70) Table 430.248 (single-phase AC motors) and Table 430.250 (three-phase induction motors). Per NEC 430.6(A)(1) the table values (not nameplate) are used for conductors and short-circuit/ground-fault protection; conductors for one motor ≥ 125 % of the table current (430.22). Overload protection uses the nameplate current. Check against the NEC edition in force.
```

**Inputs:** Phases, Motor rating, Motor rating, Voltage, Voltage

**Results:** Full-load current (table) [A], Conductor design current 125 % [A], Power [kW]

### Connection & starting

#### Star-delta starting

*Starting current, torque, overload setting* — `star_delta`

```text
In star: I_A,Y = I_A,Δ / 3    M_A,Y = M_A,Δ / 3
Overload relay in the motor phase (after main contactor): setting I_N / √3 ≈ 0.58 · I_N.
Requires a motor rated for delta at mains voltage (e.g. 400 V Δ / 690 V Y).
```

**Inputs:** Rated current I_N [A], Starting current ratio I_A/I_N, Starting torque ratio M_A/M_N, Load torque at start M_L/M_N

**Results:** Starting current DOL (Δ) [A], Starting current star (Y) [A], Starting torque star M_A,Y / M_N, Overload relay setting [A]

#### 3~ motor on single phase (Steinmetz)

*Run and start capacitor* — `steinmetz`

```text
Rule of thumb: C_run ≈ 70 µF per kW at 230 V / 50 Hz (scaled by (230/U)² · 50/f)
C_start ≈ 2…3 · C_run (during start only). Output approx. 70…80 % of P_N.
Connect motor in delta for 230 V (230/400 V motor). Capacitor rated voltage ≥ 1.25 · U.
```

**Inputs:** Motor output P_N [kW], Single-phase voltage [V], Frequency f [Hz]

**Results:** Run capacitor C_run [µF], Start capacitor C_start, Available output approx. [kW]

#### Three-phase motor connection (6 terminals)

*Star or delta connection on the terminal board (U1…W2)* — `motor_wiring_6`

```text
Terminal marking per IEC 60034-8 (old DIN VDE 0530: U, V, W / X, Y, Z). Top W2 U2 V2, bottom U1 V1 W1.
Star (Y): bridge W2–U2–V2, supply at U1/V1/W1; each winding sees U/√3.
Delta (Δ): bridges W2–U1, U2–V1, V2–W1; each winding sees U.
Rating plate 230/400 V on a 400 V system → star; 400/690 V on a 400 V system → delta (also for star-delta starting).
L1→U1, L2→V1, L3→W1 = clockwise (looking at the shaft end).
```

**Inputs:** System voltage (line) [V], Rating plate, Show

**Results:** Connection, Bridges, Supply, Voltage per winding [V]

#### Three-phase motor connection (9 leads)

*NEMA T1–T9, wye or delta, high/low voltage* — `motor_wiring_9`

```text
Lead marking per NEMA MG 1 (North America), dual-voltage motors e.g. 230/460 V: high voltage = winding halves in series, low voltage = parallel.
9 leads: wye (internal star point of T7-T8-T9) or delta (internal delta corners T1, T2, T3).
12 leads: all winding ends brought out; wye or delta selectable (also wye-delta or part-winding starting). The manufacturer's nameplate and connection diagram take precedence.
```

**Inputs:** Connection, Voltage

**Results:** L1, L2, L3, Tie together (insulated)

#### Three-phase motor connection (12 leads)

*NEMA T1–T12, wye/delta, series/parallel* — `motor_wiring_12`

```text
Lead marking per NEMA MG 1 (North America), dual-voltage motors e.g. 230/460 V: high voltage = winding halves in series, low voltage = parallel.
9 leads: wye (internal star point of T7-T8-T9) or delta (internal delta corners T1, T2, T3).
12 leads: all winding ends brought out; wye or delta selectable (also wye-delta or part-winding starting). The manufacturer's nameplate and connection diagram take precedence.
```

**Inputs:** Connection, Voltage

**Results:** L1, L2, L3, Tie together (insulated)

#### Motor connections (direction of rotation)

*Connection and reversing of three-phase and capacitor motors* — `motor_connections`

```text
Three-phase (IEC 60034-8): L1→U1, L2→V1, L3→W1 gives clockwise rotation (looking at the shaft end, clockwise field). Counter-clockwise: swap two lines (e.g. L1↔L2).
Single-phase capacitor motor: main winding U1–U2 across L–N, auxiliary winding Z1–Z2 in parallel via the run capacitor C_B. Reversing: swap the auxiliary winding (Z1↔Z2) – the actual direction is given by the manufacturer's diagram.
```

**Inputs:** Motor, Direction

**Results:** Connection, Reversing

#### Motor terminal markings

| Machine / winding | IEC 60034-8 | Old (DIN VDE 0530) | NEMA MG 1 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Three-phase, 6 terminals | U1 V1 W1 / U2 V2 W2 | U V W / X Y Z | T1 T2 T3 / T4 T5 T6 |
| Three-phase, 9 leads | – | – | T1 … T9 |
| Three-phase, 12 leads | U1 U2 U5 U6, V…, W… | – | T1 … T12 |
| Dahlander (2 speeds) | 1U 1V 1W / 2U 2V 2W | – | – |
| Two separate windings | 1U1 … 1W2 / 2U1 … 2W2 | – | – |
| Single-phase: main/auxiliary winding | U1–U2 / Z1–Z2 | – | T1–T4 / T5, T8 |
| DC: armature | A1–A2 | A–B | A1–A2 |
| DC: shunt field | E1–E2 | C–D | F1–F2 |
| DC: series field | D1–D2 | E–F | S1–S2 |
| DC: interpole | B1–B2 | G–H | – |
| DC: separate excitation | F1–F2 | J–K | F1–F2 |

*IEC 60034-8: digit 1 = start, 2 = end of winding; prefixed digit = speed step or winding. Old marking per DIN VDE 0530 (until 1977). NEMA MG 1: T numbers. The nameplate takes precedence.*

## Electronics & components

### Components & codes

#### Colour code resistor / inductor

*Colour bands → value and value → colour bands (4 and 5 bands, IEC 60062) with drawing* — `resistor_color`

```text
4 bands: digit, digit, multiplier, tolerance (E6–E24)
5 bands: digit, digit, digit, multiplier, tolerance (E48–E192)
Black 0 ×1 · brown 1 ×10 ±1 % · red 2 ×100 ±2 % · orange 3 ×1 k · yellow 4 ×10 k · green 5 ×100 k ±0.5 % · blue 6 ×1 M ±0.25 % · violet 7 ×10 M ±0.1 % · grey 8 ×100 M ±0.05 % · white 9 ×1 G · gold ×0.1 ±5 % · silver ×0.01 ±10 % · no band ±20 %
Value → colour bands: rounded to 2 or 3 significant digits and checked against E24 or E96. Inductors: same code, base unit µH.
```

**Inputs:** Direction, Component, Resistance, Unit, Number of bands, 1st band (digit), 2nd band (digit), 3rd band (digit), Multiplier, Tolerance

**Results:** Nominal value [Ω], Colour bands, Tolerance [%], Minimum [Ω], Maximum [Ω], Nearest standard value [Ω], Standard series

#### E series: find standard value

*Nearest value in E6 … E96 (IEC 60063)* — `e_series`

```text
Finds the nearest standard value (logarithmic distance) plus the next lower and higher value.
```

**Inputs:** Value [Ω], Series

**Results:** Nearest standard value [Ω], Deviation [%], Next lower [Ω], Next higher [Ω]

#### SMD resistor code

*3 and 4 digits, R notation and EIA-96* — `smd_resistor`

```text
3 digits (E24): two digits + power of ten, e.g. 472 = 47 · 10² = 4.7 kΩ
4 digits (E96, 1 %): three digits + power of ten, e.g. 4702 = 47 kΩ
R = decimal point: 4R7 = 4.7 Ω, R47 = 0.47 Ω; 0 / 000 = 0 Ω jumper
EIA-96 (1 %): two digits = index of the E96 series (01 = 100 … 96 = 976) + letter: Z ×0.001, Y/R ×0.01, X/S ×0.1, A ×1, B/H ×10, C ×100, D ×1000, E ×10⁴, F ×10⁵. Example 01C = 10 kΩ.
```

**Inputs:** Marking

**Results:** Resistance [Ω], Code type

#### Capacitor code

*Marking → capacitance, tolerance, voltage* — `capacitor_code`

```text
3 digits (pF): two digits + power of ten, 104 = 10 · 10⁴ pF = 100 nF; 8 = ×0.01, 9 = ×0.1.
Letter as decimal point: 4n7 = 4.7 nF, 47p = 47 pF, 2u2 = 2.2 µF, 1R5 = 1.5 pF; decimal number (0.1, .047) = µF.
Tolerance: B ±0.1 pF, C ±0.25 pF, D ±0.5 pF, F ±1 %, G ±2 %, J ±5 %, K ±10 %, M ±20 %, Z +80/−20 %.
Voltage (EIA, prefixed): e.g. 1H = 50 V, 2A = 100 V, 2J = 630 V.
```

**Inputs:** Marking

**Results:** Capacitance [nF], Capacitance in pF [pF], Tolerance, Rated voltage [V]

| 224 | 220000 | 220 | 0.22 |
| 334 | 330000 | 330 | 0.33 |
| 474 | 470000 | 470 | 0.47 |
| 684 | 680000 | 680 | 0.68 |
| 105 | 1000000 | 1000 | 1 |
| 155 | 1500000 | 1500 | 1.5 |
| 225 | 2200000 | 2200 | 2.2 |
| 335 | 3300000 | 3300 | 3.3 |
| 475 | 4700000 | 4700 | 4.7 |
| 685 | 6800000 | 6800 | 6.8 |

*Two digits + number of zeros in pF (EIA/IEC 60062). Tolerance letter after: J ±5 %, K ±10 %, M ±20 %. Other values see the capacitor code calculator.*

#### Capacitor voltage codes (EIA)

| Code | Rated voltage in V |
|---|---|
| 0G | 4 |
| 0L | 5.5 |
| 0J | 6.3 |
| 1A | 10 |
| 1C | 16 |
| 1D | 20 |
| 1E | 25 |
| 1V | 35 |
| 1H | 50 |
| 1J | 63 |
| 1K | 80 |
| 2A | 100 |
| 2B | 125 |
| 2C | 160 |
| 2D | 200 |
| 2E | 250 |
| 2F | 315 |
| 2V | 350 |
| 2G | 400 |
| 2W | 450 |
| 2J | 630 |
| 3A | 1000 |

*EIA voltage code on film, ceramic and electrolytic capacitors (e.g. 2A104J = 100 V, 100 nF, ±5 %).*

#### Alphanumeric inductor code

*101 = 100 µH, 4R7 = 4.7 µH, 47N = 47 nH* — `inductor_code`

```text
Base unit µH: two digits + power of ten (101 = 10 · 10¹ µH = 100 µH); R = decimal point in µH (4R7 = 4.7 µH, R10 = 0.1 µH); N = decimal point in nH (47N = 47 nH, 4N7 = 4.7 nH). Suffix tolerance J ±5 %, K ±10 %, M ±20 %.
```

**Inputs:** Marking

**Results:** Inductance [µH], Inductance in nH [nH]

### Circuits

#### Voltage divider

*U_out unloaded and loaded* — `voltage_divider`

```text
U_out = U_in · R2 / (R1 + R2)
Loaded: R2 ∥ R_L instead of R2.
```

**Inputs:** Input voltage U_in [V], R1 [kΩ], R2 [kΩ], Load R_L [kΩ]

**Results:** U_out unloaded [V], U_out loaded [V], Divider current [mA], Divider ratio, Power R1 [mW]

#### Series resistor (LED and load)

*R = (U − n · U_F) / I_F or (U − U_L) / I_L with standard value and power* — `series_resistor`

```text
LED: R = (U − n · U_F) / I_F, the next higher E24 value is selected (current stays ≤ I_F). Typ. U_F: red 1.8–2.1 V, green 2.0–3.2 V, blue/white 2.8–3.4 V.
Load: R_v = (U − U_L) / I_L, nearest E12 value; U_L with this value for a resistive load. Only for a constant load – with varying current use a voltage regulator.
P_R = I² · R, choose a rating ≥ 2 · P_R. Efficiency η = U_load / U.
```

**Inputs:** Load, Supply voltage U [V], Forward voltage U_F [V], LED current I_F [mA], LEDs in series, Voltage at the load U_L [V], Load current I_L [mA]

**Results:** Calculated resistance [Ω], Selected standard value [Ω], Current with standard value [mA], Load voltage with standard value [V], Power P_R [W], Recommended rating ≥ [W], Efficiency [%]

#### Zener diode as voltage regulator

*Series resistor and Zener diode dissipation* — `zener_regulator`

```text
R_v = (U_in,min − U_Z) / (I_Z,min + I_L,max)  →  next lower E12 value
I_Z,max = (U_in,max − U_Z) / R_v − I_L,min    P_Z = U_Z · I_Z,max    P_R = (U_in,max − U_Z)² / R_v
I_Z,min typ. 5 mA (or 10 % of I_Z,max). Choose the Zener by P_Z with margin (e.g. 0.5 W, 1.3 W).
```

**Inputs:** Input voltage min. [V], Input voltage max. [V], Zener voltage U_Z [V], Min. Zener current I_Z,min [mA], Load current min. [mA], Load current max. [mA], Zener power rating [W]

**Results:** Series resistor (E12) [Ω], Series resistor calculated [Ω], Power in R_v [W], Max. Zener current [mA], Max. Zener dissipation [W]

#### RC circuit

*τ, cut-off frequency, charging, energy* — `rc`

```text
τ = R · C    f_c = 1 / (2π · R · C)    u_C(t) = U_0 · (1 − e^(−t/τ))
After 5τ ≈ 99.3 % charged.    E = ½ · C · U²    Q = C · U
```

**Inputs:** Resistance R [kΩ], Capacitance C [µF], Charging voltage U_0 [V], Time t [ms]

**Results:** Time constant τ [ms], 5 τ [ms], Cut-off frequency f_c [Hz], Voltage u_C(t) [V], Stored energy [mJ], Charge Q [µC]

#### LC resonance

*Resonant frequency f₀ = 1 / (2π√(LC))* — `lc_resonance`

```text
f₀ = 1 / (2π · √(L · C))    Z₀ = √(L / C)
```

**Inputs:** Inductance L [mH], Capacitance C [µF]

**Results:** Resonant frequency f₀ [Hz], Angular frequency ω₀ [1/s], Characteristic impedance Z₀ [Ω]

#### Reactance & impedance (series RLC)

*X_L, X_C, Z and φ at frequency f* — `reactance`

```text
X_L = 2π · f · L    X_C = 1 / (2π · f · C)    Z = √(R² + (X_L − X_C)²)    φ = arctan((X_L − X_C)/R)
Empty fields = component not present.
```

**Inputs:** Frequency f [Hz], Resistance R [Ω], Inductance L [mH], Capacitance C [µF]

**Results:** Inductive reactance X_L [Ω], Capacitive reactance X_C [Ω], Impedance Z [Ω], Phase angle φ [°]

#### Battery / UPS runtime

*Backup time from Ah, V and load* — `battery_runtime`

```text
E_usable = U · C · DoD    t = E_usable · η / P_load
Simplified, without Peukert effect and ageing – check manufacturer curves.
```

**Inputs:** Battery voltage [V], Capacity [Ah], Load power [W], Converter efficiency [%], Usable depth of discharge [%]

**Results:** Usable energy [Wh], Battery current [A], Runtime [h], Runtime [min]

## Building automation & I&C

### Sensors & signals

#### Analog signal scaling

*4–20 mA / 0–10 V ↔ value ↔ raw (PLC)* — `analog_scaling`

```text
Linear scaling: y = y_min + (x − x_min) · (y_max − y_min) / (x_max − x_min)
Raw values: Siemens S7 0…27648 (nominal), 12 bit 0…4095, 15 bit 0…32767, 16 bit 0…65535.
NAMUR NE 43 (4–20 mA): measuring range 3.8…20.5 mA, failure ≤ 3.6 mA or ≥ 21 mA.
```

**Inputs:** Signal, Given, Value, Range start, Range end, Raw format

**Results:** Percentage of span [%], Signal [mA], Engineering value, Raw value

#### 4–20 mA loop burden

*Max. load, cable resistance, max. length* — `loop_burden`

```text
R_max = (U_S − U_min,transmitter) / I_max
R_cable = 2 · ρ · L / A    Margin = R_max − R_load − R_cable
Typical: U_S = 24 V, transmitter 10…12 V, AI burden 250 Ω (HART ≥ 230 Ω). I_max 20 mA (or 21…22 mA for NE 43 failure signal).
```

**Inputs:** Supply voltage U_S [V], Min. transmitter voltage [V], Max. loop current [mA], Load (AI, indicator …) [Ω], Cable length (one way) [m], Cross-section [mm²]

**Results:** Max. total load [Ω], Cable resistance (loop) [Ω], Margin [Ω], Max. cable length [m]

#### RTD Pt/Ni/Cu

*Temperature ↔ resistance for Pt100/Pt1000/Ni1000/Cu100* — `rtd`

```text
Pt (IEC 60751): R = R0 · (1 + A·T + B·T² + C·(T − 100)·T³), C below 0 °C only
A = 3.9083·10⁻³, B = −5.775·10⁻⁷, C = −4.183·10⁻¹²
Ni1000 (DIN 43760): R = R0 · (1 + A·T + B·T² + D·T⁴ + F·T⁶)
Ni1000 TK5000: R = R0 · (1 + 4.427·10⁻³·T + 5.172·10⁻⁶·T² + 5.585·10⁻⁹·T³)
Cu10/Cu50/Cu100: R = R0 · (1 + α · T), linear −50 … 150 °C; α = 0.00427 1/K (Cu10, US practice), 0.00428 1/K (GOST 6651)
```

**Inputs:** Sensor type, Given, Temperature T [°C], Resistance R [Ω]

**Results:** Resistance R [Ω], Temperature T [°C], Sensitivity dR/dT [Ω/K]

#### RTD lead wire error

*Error from leads in 2-wire connection* — `rtd_wire_error`

```text
2-wire: lead resistance R_L = 2 · ρ · L / A adds to the sensor.
Error ΔT = R_L / (dR/dT). 3-wire compensates with equal leads, 4-wire completely.
Use Pt100 2-wire only with very short leads; BMS usually uses Pt1000/Ni1000.
```

**Inputs:** Sensor type, Connection, Cable length (one way) [m], Cross-section [mm²], Measured temperature [°C]

**Results:** Lead resistance (loop) [Ω], Measurement error ΔT [K]

#### NTC thermistor (beta model)

*Temperature ↔ resistance for NTC 10k, 20k …* — `ntc`

```text
R(T) = R25 · exp(β · (1/T − 1/298.15 K))
Typical: NTC10k β = 3435 K or 3950 K, NTC20k β = 4250 K (check datasheet!).
```

**Inputs:** Nominal resistance R25 [kΩ], Beta value (B25/85) [K], Given, Temperature T [°C], Resistance R [kΩ]

**Results:** Resistance R [kΩ], Temperature T [°C]

#### Thermocouples (type K, J, T, N, E, R, S, B)

*Thermal EMF ↔ temperature with reference junction* — `thermocouple`

```text
Reference values per IEC 60584-1 (= NIST ITS-90 reference functions, reference junction 0 °C).
Measured voltage U = E(θ_M) − E(θ_R) with θ_R = reference (terminal) temperature. Inverse: E(θ_M) = U + E(θ_R).
Extension cable colours per IEC 60584-3: K green, J black, T brown, N pink, E violet, R/S orange, B grey (plus conductor coloured, minus white).
```

**Inputs:** Type, Find, Measuring temperature θ_M [°C], Measured voltage U [mV], Reference junction temperature θ_R [°C]

**Results:** Thermal EMF U [mV], Measuring temperature θ_M [°C], E(θ_M) referred to 0 °C [mV], E(θ_R) of the reference junction [mV], Sensitivity at θ_M [µV/K]

#### Thermocouples: colours and data

| Type | Pair | Range | E(100 °C) in mV | IEC 60584-3 | ANSI MC96.1 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| K | NiCr – NiAl | -270 … 1372 °C | 4.096 | green / white | yellow / red, jacket yellow |
| J | Fe – CuNi | -210 … 1200 °C | 5.269 | black / white | white / red, jacket black |
| T | Cu – CuNi | -270 … 400 °C | 4.279 | brown / white | blue / red, jacket blue |
| E | NiCr – CuNi | -270 … 1000 °C | 6.319 | violet / white | purple / red, jacket purple |
| N | NiCrSi – NiSi | -270 … 1300 °C | 2.774 | pink / white | orange / red, jacket orange |
| R | Pt13Rh – Pt | -50 … 1768 °C | 0.647 | orange / white | black / red, jacket green |
| S | Pt10Rh – Pt | -50 … 1768 °C | 0.646 | orange / white | black / red, jacket green |
| B | Pt30Rh – Pt6Rh | 0 … 1820 °C | 0.033 | grey / white | grey / red, jacket grey |

*Pair (plus – minus), defined range of the reference values (IEC 60584-1 / NIST ITS-90), EMF at 100 °C (reference junction 0 °C), colours plus/minus of extension cable per IEC 60584-3 (sheath as plus) and ANSI/ASTM MC96.1 (minus always red). Usable range smaller depending on wire size. Calculator “Thermocouples”.*

#### 24 V supply sizing

*Transformer (AC) or PSU (DC) for field devices* — `psu_24v`

```text
P_req = Σ(n · P) · g · (1 + margin)
Size actuators by their dimensioning power (VA) from the datasheet, not operating power. AC transformer sizes: 25 … 1000 VA; DC PSUs 2.5 … 40 A at 24 V.
```

**Inputs:** Supply, Quantity group 1, Power per device gr. 1 [VA / W], Quantity group 2, Power per device gr. 2 [VA / W], Quantity group 3, Power per device gr. 3 [VA / W], Simultaneity factor g, Margin [%]

**Results:** Connected total [VA], Required [VA], Recommended standard size [VA], Current at 24 V (standard size) [A]

### Control & bus systems

#### Controller tuning (Ziegler/Nichols, CHR)

*Kp, Tn, Tv from process data Ks, Tu, Tg – difficulty S* — `controller_tuning`

```text
Difficulty S = Tu / Tg (or Tt / T): < 0.1 easy, ≈ 0.2 medium, > 0.3 hard to control.
Ziegler/Nichols (1942, max. 20 % overshoot; single-capacity process with dead time Tt, time constant T):
P: Kp = T/(Ks·Tt) · PI: 0.9·T/(Ks·Tt), Tn = 3.3·Tt · PD: 1.2·T/(Ks·Tt), Tv = 0.25·Tt · PID: 1.2·T/(Ks·Tt), Tn = 2·Tt, Tv = 0.42·Tt.
Chien/Hrones/Reswick (1952, multi-capacity process, V0 = Ks·Kp):
aperiodic – disturbance: P 0.3; PI 0.6 / Tn 4·Tu; PID 0.95 / Tn 2.4·Tu / Tv 0.42·Tu – set point: P 0.3; PI 0.35 / Tn 1.2·Tg; PID 0.6 / Tn Tg / Tv 0.5·Tu;
20 % overshoot – disturbance: P 0.7; PI 0.7 / Tn 2.3·Tu; PID 1.2 / Tn 2·Tu / Tv 0.42·Tu – set point: P 0.7; PI 0.6 / Tn Tg; PID 0.95 / Tn 1.35·Tg / Tv 0.47·Tu (V0 factors each · Tg/Tu).
Source: Siemens “Regeltechnik – Grundlagen” (08/2022), ch. 3.3 and 6.5, p. 33–34 and 82–83 (Fig. 6-15, 6-18). Starting values – fine-tune on site.
```

**Inputs:** Process gain Ks, Delay time Tu or dead time Tt [s], Balance time Tg or time constant T [s], Controller type, Tuning rule

**Results:** Difficulty S, Controllability, Proportional gain Kp, Proportional band Xp = 100 %/Kp [%], Integral time Tn [s], Derivative time Tv [s]

#### KNX topology & line planning

*Lines, areas, segments, cable lengths and PSU load (KNX TP)* — `knx_topology`

```text
KNX TP: up to 64 devices per line segment with its own power supply; with line repeaters up to 4 segments = 255 devices per line; up to 15 lines per area (line couplers), up to 15 areas (area couplers); couplers count on the main/backbone line.
Cable lengths per segment: power supply – device ≤ 350 m, device – device ≤ 700 m, total ≤ 1000 m.
Practice: start a new line from about 64 devices and plan spare capacity.
Source: KNX Association “KNX basic knowledge” (2013), p. 8–9. Current per device from the manufacturer.
```

**Inputs:** Number of bus devices, Spare capacity [%], Devices per line (planning), Cable length per segment (total) [m], Largest distance PSU – device [m], Largest distance device – device [m], Current per device [mA], Power supply

**Results:** Lines, Areas, Devices per line (max.), Segments per line, Power supplies (lines), PSU load per segment [%]

#### DALI line (voltage drop)

*Cross-section for max. 2 V drop* — `dali_bus`

```text
DALI (IEC 62386): max. 2 V drop on the bus line, max. 250 mA, max. 300 m.
A_min = 2 · L · I · ρ / ΔU_max
Guide values: up to 100 m 0.5 mm², 150 m 0.75 mm², 300 m 1.5 mm².
```

**Inputs:** Line length (one way) [m], Bus current [mA], Max. voltage drop [V]

**Results:** Min. cross-section [mm²], Recommended cross-section [mm²], Voltage drop at recommendation [V]

#### Fieldbuses: lengths & nodes

| Bus | Physical | Max. length | Nodes | Data rate | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BACnet MS/TP | RS-485 | 1200 m per segment | 32 unit loads/segment, max. 128 devices per bus, MAC 0–127 (manager) | 9,6–115,2 kbit/s | 120 Ω both ends, bias once; > 750 m possibly 76.8 kbit/s |
| Modbus RTU | RS-485 | approx. 1000–1200 m (baud-dependent) | 32/segment, addresses 1–247 | 9,6–115,2 kbit/s | 120 Ω both ends; check A/B naming |
| KNX TP1 | 2-wire, free topology | line 1000 m; 350 m PSU–device; 700 m device–device | 64 per line segment; 4 segments = 255 per line; 15 lines × 15 areas | 9,6 kbit/s | SELV 30 V DC, no termination |
| DALI | 2-wire, free | 300 m | 64 control gear | 1,2 kbit/s | max. 2 V drop, 250 mA |
| LON FT-10 | 2-wire, free or bus | free 500 m, bus 2700 m | 64 per segment | 78 kbit/s | terminator: free 1×, bus 2× |
| PROFIBUS DP | RS-485 | 1200 m (≤ 93.75 kbit/s) … 100 m (12 Mbit/s) | 32/segment, 126 addresses | 9,6 kbit/s – 12 Mbit/s | active termination both ends |
| CAN / CANopen | 2-wire, line | 25 m (1 Mbit/s) … 1000 m (50 kbit/s) | up to 127 (CANopen) | 10 kbit/s – 1 Mbit/s | 120 Ω both ends |
| IO-Link | point-to-point, 3/5-wire | 20 m | 1 device per port | 4,8/38,4/230,4 kbit/s | unshielded standard cable, M12 |
| Ethernet (BACnet/IP, Modbus TCP, PROFINET) | twisted pair, star | 100 m per copper link | – | 100 Mbit/s + | Cat 5e or better |

*Typical limits without repeaters per the respective specification – check manufacturer data and cable type. Checked against Siemens “Ethernet, TCP/IP, MS/TP and BACnet” (2023), ch. 7.1, and KNX Association “KNX basic knowledge” (2013), p. 8–9.*

### Data points & coding

#### AMEV data point address

*User address per AMEV GA 2005 from trade, plant, component, data point* — `amev_address`

```text
Structure (25 characters + plain text): site (5) – building (3) – trade (1) – plant (3) – no. (2) – controller make (2) – component (3) – no. (2) – point type (2) – point description (2).
Example: 31021-008-H-HKR-01-01-PUU-01-SM-17 = heating circuit 01, circulation pump 01, fault message “general electrical fault”.
Source: AMEV “Gebäudeautomation 2005” (no. 87), appendix 2.1/2.2, p. 78–85. Terms as in the original (German only); adaptable per project, check newer AMEV recommendations (e.g. BACnet).
```

**Inputs:** Site number, Building no. (999 = outdoor), Trade, Plant, Plant no., Controller make (no.), Component, Component no., Point type, Point description

**Results:** Data point address, Without separators (BMS), Plain text (suggestion, max. 30 characters)
