### BAC efficiency

#### BAC efficiency class (EN ISO 52120)

*Savings from better building automation – BAC factors per building type* — `bac_efficiency`

```text
Energy new = energy old · f_BAC,new / f_BAC,old;  saving = 1 − f_new / f_old
f_BAC,th: heating and cooling; f_BAC,el: lighting and auxiliary energy (no other appliances).
Class C = 1 (standard reference), D worse, B and A better.
Source: Siemens “Building automation – impact on energy efficiency, application of EN ISO 52120” (09/2022), ch. 4.4, p. 124–125. Check against the edition of EN ISO 52120-1 in force.
```

**Inputs:** Building type, BAC class today, BAC class planned, Thermal energy today [kWh/a], Electric energy today [kWh/a], Price heat/cooling [€ / CHF], Price electricity [€ / CHF]

**Results:** f_BAC,th today, f_BAC,th planned, Saving thermal [%], Thermal energy planned [kWh/a], f_BAC,el today, f_BAC,el planned, Saving electric [%], Electric energy planned [kWh/a], Cost saving per year [€]

#### BAC efficiency factors f_BAC (EN ISO 52120)

| Building type | thermal D / C / B / A | electric D / C / B / A |
|---|---|---|
| Office | 1.51 / 1 / 0.8 / 0.7 | 1.1 / 1 / 0.93 / 0.87 |
| Lecture hall | 1.24 / 1 / 0.75 / 0.5 a) | 1.06 / 1 / 0.94 / 0.89 |
| Education (school) | 1.2 / 1 / 0.88 / 0.8 | 1.07 / 1 / 0.93 / 0.86 |
| Hospital | 1.31 / 1 / 0.91 / 0.86 | 1.05 / 1 / 0.98 / 0.96 |
| Hotel | 1.31 / 1 / 0.85 / 0.68 | 1.07 / 1 / 0.95 / 0.9 |
| Restaurant | 1.23 / 1 / 0.77 / 0.68 | 1.04 / 1 / 0.96 / 0.92 |
| Wholesale and retail | 1.56 / 1 / 0.73 / 0.6 a) | 1.08 / 1 / 0.95 / 0.91 |
| Sports, storage, industry etc. | 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 | 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 |
| Residential | 1.1 / 1 / 0.88 / 0.81 | 1.08 / 1 / 0.93 / 0.92 |

*Class C = 1 (reference). a) value depends strongly on the ventilation heating/cooling demand.
Source: Siemens “Building automation – impact on energy efficiency, application of EN ISO 52120” (09/2022), ch. 4.4, p. 124–125. Check against the edition of EN ISO 52120-1 in force.*

## HVAC & plumbing

### Air & climate

#### h,x chart (Mollier) & air processes

*Dew point, humidity, heating/cooling/humidifying/mixing in the chart* — `hx_diagram`

```text
Air pressure from the standard atmosphere: p = 1013.25 · (1 − 0.0065 · H / 288.15)^5.255 hPa
p_s by Magnus (WMO, over water): p_s = 6.112 · e^(17.62·t / (243.12 + t)) hPa    p_v = φ · p_s
x = 0.622 · p_v / (p − p_v)    h = 1.006 · t + x · (2501 + 1.86 · t) kJ/kg dry air
Dew point τ from p_v (inverse Magnus); wet bulb t_wb: h + (x_s(t_wb) − x) · 4.19 · t_wb = h_s(t_wb)
Heating/cooling: x = const.; below the dew point ideally saturated (x₂ = x_s(t₂), condensate ṁ · Δx)
Steam humidifier: t ≈ const.; evaporative humidifier: h = const.; mixing: x and h mass weighted
Power Q = ṁ · Δh, ṁ = V̇ · ρ₁ / 3600. Chart: oblique h,x coordinates (0 °C isotherm horizontal).
Below 0 °C saturation over water is used. Real coils: consider bypass factor / surface temperature.
```

**Inputs:** Altitude above sea level [m], Temperature t₁ [°C], Given, Relative humidity φ₁ [%], Dew point τ₁ [°C], Type, Target temperature t₂ [°C], Target humidity φ₂ [%], Temperature state B [°C], Relative humidity state B [%], Share of B in mass flow [%], Airflow V̇ (at state 1) [m³/h]

**Results:** Air pressure p [hPa], Humidity ratio x₁ [g/kg], Dew point τ₁ [°C], Enthalpy h₁ [kJ/kg], Wet-bulb temperature t_wb,1 [°C], Relative humidity φ₁ [%], Vapour pressure p_v,1 [hPa], Saturation pressure p_s,1 [hPa], Density ρ₁ [kg/m³], Temperature t₂ [°C], Relative humidity φ₂ [%], Humidity ratio x₂ [g/kg], Enthalpy h₂ [kJ/kg], Dew point τ₂ [°C], Enthalpy difference Δh [kJ/kg], Power Q (+ heating / − cooling) [kW], Water (+ humidifying / − condensate) [kg/h]

#### h,x chart: altitude correction

| Altitude | p (book) | k (book) | p ISA | k ISA |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 m | 1013 mbar | 1 | 1013 mbar | 1 |
| 200 m | 989 mbar | 0.976 | 989 mbar | 0.977 |
| 400 m | 966 mbar | 0.953 | 966 mbar | 0.953 |
| 600 m | 943 mbar | 0.931 | 943 mbar | 0.931 |
| 800 m | 921 mbar | 0.901 | 921 mbar | 0.909 |
| 1000 m | 899 mbar | 0.887 | 899 mbar | 0.887 |
| 1500 m | 842 mbar | 0.831 | 846 mbar | 0.835 |
| 2000 m | 795 mbar | 0.785 | 795 mbar | 0.785 |

*Book values: Siemens “h,x-Diagramm – Aufbau und Anwendung” (08/2022), p. 59. Column ISA: standard atmosphere as used by the h,x calculator. At 800 m the book gives k = 0.901; 921/1013 mbar gives 0.909 – probably a misprint. At 1500 m the book value is about 0.5 % below the standard atmosphere.*

#### Outdoor air flow rate

*Per EN 16798-1 (persons + area) or CO₂ balance* — `fresh_air`

```text
EN 16798-1 (Annex B, method 1): q = n · q_P + A · q_B
q_P per person: cat. I 10, II 7, III 4 l/s.   q_B per m² (low-polluting building): I 1.0, II 0.7, III 0.4 l/(s·m²); very low-polluting: 0.5 / 0.35 / 0.2; non-low-polluting: 2.0 / 1.4 / 0.8.
CO₂ balance (steady state): V̇ = n · G / (C_set − C_outdoor), G ≈ 20 l/h per person for office work.
Outdoor air ≈ 400 ppm, in cities often 550 … 700 ppm (Siemens “Regeln und Steuern von Lüftungsanlagen”, 10/2022, ch. 3.3.1); demand-controlled ventilation via CO₂/VOC see VDMA 24773.
```

**Inputs:** Method, Persons, Floor area [m²], Room height (for air change) [m], Category, Building pollution, CO₂ emission per person [l/h], CO₂ set point [ppm], CO₂ outdoor [ppm]

**Results:** Outdoor airflow [m³/h], Outdoor airflow [l/s], Per person [m³/h], Air change rate [1/h], CO₂ concentration (steady state) [ppm]

#### CO₂ concentrations & guide values

| CO₂ in ppm | Meaning |
|---|---|
| 400 – 450 | Normal outdoor air (urban often 550 … 700) |
| 1000 | 20 % of people entering dissatisfied (Pettenkofer) |
| 1500 | Limit per DIN 1946 (as in the book) |
| 2000 | Sensitive people complain of headache |
| 4000 | Peak values in classrooms after lessons |
| 5000 | Occupational exposure limit |
| 100000 | Candle goes out, loss of consciousness |

*Source: Siemens “Regeln und Steuern von Lüftungsanlagen” (10/2022), Fig. 3-1, p. 28. DIN 1946 and MAK values as given in the book – check current rules (e.g. ASR A3.6, UBA, TRGS 900).*

#### Air handling unit & duct

*Velocity class (EN 13053), SFP class, duct diameter* — `ahu_sizing`

```text
Face velocity v = V̇ / (3600 · W · H) in the free unit cross-section.
Velocity classes EN 13053: V1 ≤ 1.6 · V2 ≤ 1.8 · V3 ≤ 2.0 · V4 ≤ 2.2 · V5 ≤ 2.5 m/s (above: V6 …).
SFP = P_el / V̇ in W/(m³/s); classes EN 13779 / EN 16798-3: SFP 1 ≤ 500, 2 ≤ 750, 3 ≤ 1250, 4 ≤ 2000, 5 ≤ 3000, 6 ≤ 4500, 7 > 4500.
Round duct d = √(4 · V̇ / (3600 · π · v)); low-velocity systems < 10 m/s in the duct.
Sources: TROX “Planungshandbuch RLT-Geräte” (2018), p. 6; Siemens “Einführung in die HLK- und Gebäudetechnik” (08/2022), ch. 6. Check the editions in force.
```

**Inputs:** Air flow V̇ [m³/h], Unit inner cross-section: width [m], Unit inner cross-section: height [m], Fan electric power [kW], Duct velocity [m/s]

**Results:** Face velocity [m/s], Velocity class, Specific fan power SFP [W/(m³/s)], SFP class, Round duct diameter [mm], Duct cross-section [m²]

#### AHU: velocity and SFP classes

| Class | Limit |
|---|---|
| V1 | ≤ 1,6 m/s |
| V2 | 1,6 – 1,8 m/s |
| V3 | 1,8 – 2,0 m/s |
| V4 | 2,0 – 2,2 m/s |
| V5 | 2,2 – 2,5 m/s |
| SFP 1 | ≤ 500 W/(m³/s) |
| SFP 2 | 500 – 750 W/(m³/s) |
| SFP 3 | 750 – 1250 W/(m³/s) |
| SFP 4 | 1250 – 2000 W/(m³/s) |
| SFP 5 | 2000 – 3000 W/(m³/s) |
| SFP 6 | 3000 – 4500 W/(m³/s) |
| SFP 7 | > 4500 W/(m³/s) |

*Sources: TROX “Planungshandbuch RLT-Geräte” (2018), p. 6 (V2–V5); SFP classes per EN 13779:2007 (adopted in EN 16798-3). Check the edition in force.*

### Heating & heat pumps

#### Heat output ↔ flow rate

*Water or air: P = V̇ · ρ · c · ΔT* — `heat_flow`

```text
P = V̇ · ρ · c · ΔT
Water: ρ = 1000 kg/m³, c = 4.187 kJ/(kg·K) → V̇ [m³/h] = P [kW] / (1.163 · ΔT)
Air: ρ = 1.2 kg/m³, c = 1.005 kJ/(kg·K) → P [W] ≈ 0.335 · V̇ [m³/h] · ΔT
```

**Inputs:** Medium, Given, Power P [kW], Flow rate V̇ [m³/h], Temperature difference ΔT [K], Density ρ [kg/m³], Specific heat c [kJ/(kg·K)]

**Results:** Power P [kW], Flow rate V̇ [m³/h], Flow rate V̇ [l/s], Mass flow ṁ [kg/h]

#### Heating curve: slope & parallel shift

*Flow temperature vs. outdoor temperature, night setback* — `heating_curve`

```text
Slope s = (T_F(−5 °C) − T_F(+15 °C)) / 20 K, usual setting range 0.25 … 4.0.
Room set-point correction/setback: ΔT_F = ΔT_R · (1 + s) – parallel shift of the curve.
Support points: straight line through the points at −5 °C and +15 °C (linear grid as on the controller).
Design: T_F = T_i + (T_m,d − T_i)·q^(1/n) + (T_F,d − T_R,d)·q/2, q = (T_i − T_a)/(T_i − T_a,d), radiator exponent n ≈ 1.3 (floor heating ≈ 1.1).
Source: Siemens “Regeln und Steuern von Heizungsanlagen” (03/2023), ch. 3.1, p. 18–20 (example 67/33 °C, s = 1.7, −5 K → −13.5 K).
```

**Inputs:** Curve from, Flow at −5 °C [°C], Flow at +15 °C [°C], Room temperature (design) [°C], Design outdoor temperature [°C], Flow at design [°C], Return at design [°C], Radiator exponent n, Room set-point correction / setback [K], Current outdoor temperature [°C]

**Results:** Slope s, Flow at −5 °C [°C], Flow at +15 °C [°C], Flow shift ΔT_F [K], Shifted: flow at −5 °C [°C], Shifted: flow at +15 °C [°C], Flow set point at T_a [°C], Flow set point shifted [°C]

#### Heat pump: COP, Carnot & SPF

*COP from temperatures, minimum COP per EN 14511, seasonal performance factor* — `heat_pump`

```text
COP_Carnot = T_sink / (T_sink − T_source) (temperatures in K); real COP ≈ efficiency grade · COP_Carnot.
COP = Φ_c / P_el (instantaneous value for one operating point);  SPF = heat / electricity over a year (kWh/kWh).
Minimum COP for the Keymark label (EN 14511): air/water > 3.1 (A2/W35), brine/water > 4.3 (B0/W35), water/water > 5.1 (W10/W35). Typical SPF (Fraunhofer 2011, AZ 2): air/water 2.9, brine/water 3.9.
Source: Siemens “Kältetechnik” (08/2022), ch. 7.6, p. 112–115. The efficiency grade is an assumption – use vendor data.
```

**Inputs:** Heat source, Source temperature [°C], Flow temperature (sink) [°C], Efficiency grade (assumption), COP from data sheet (test point), Heat produced per year [kWh/a], Electricity per year [kWh/a]

**Results:** COP Carnot (ideal), COP estimated (efficiency grade), Minimum COP Keymark (test point), Seasonal performance factor SPF, Typical SPF (field)

#### Heat pumps: minimum COP & typical SPF

| Heat pump | Test point | Minimum COP | Typical SPF |
|---|---|---|---|
| Air/water | A2/W35 | > 3,1 | 2,9 |
| Brine/water | B0/W35 | > 4,3 | 3,9 |
| Water/water | W10/W35 | > 5,1 | – |

*Source: Siemens “Kältetechnik” (08/2022), p. 113–115 (SPF: Fraunhofer study 2011, AZ 2). Test point: A = air, B = brine, W = water; number = source inlet or flow temperature in °C. Check the edition in force.*

### Hydronics & valves

#### Hydraulic basic circuits (control groups)

*Schematic, function, applications and valve sizing – with selection help* — `hydraulic_circuits`

```text
Output Q̇ ~ V̇ · ΔT: flow control (variable flow: throttling, diverting) changes the flow rate, mixing control (constant flow: mixing, injection) changes the flow temperature.
Choose by distributor (pressurised: throttling, diverting, injection · unpressurised: mixing), risk of frost (constant flow), dehumidification (variable flow) and required return temperature (condensing/district heating: throttling or injection with two-way valve).
Sources: Siemens “Einführung in die HLK- und Gebäudetechnik” (08/2022), ch. 5.4–5.5, p. 144–153; Belimo selection table of hydraulic basic circuits (03/2023). Schematics without isolating and safety fittings; always check the design for the project.
```

**Inputs:** Show, Circuit, Distributor, Adjust output by, Air heater at risk of freezing, Air cooler with dehumidification, Low return temperature needed (condensing, district heating, heat pump), Consumer flow well below generator flow (surface heating), Constant flow needed in generator circuit

**Results:** Circuit, Reasoning, Alternative, Control type, Distributor, Flow temperature at consumer, Flow in consumer circuit, Flow in generator circuit, Pumps, Return temperature to generator, How it works, Characteristics, Typical applications, Valve and bypass sizing, Note

#### Selection table hydraulic basic circuits

| Circuit | Control | Distributor | Consumer flow temp. | Flow consumer / generator | Pumps | Return | Typical for |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Throttling circuit | variable flow | pressurised | = generator | Variable / Variable | Generator pump (speed control recommended) | Low at part load (heating) | Zones, radiators, coolers with dehumidification |
| Throttling circuit with PICV | variable flow | pressurised | = generator | Variable / Variable | Generator pump (speed controlled) | High at part load (cooling), low at part load (heating) | Coolers with dehumidification |
| Injection circuit with two-way valve | constant flow | pressurised | variable | Constant / Variable | Generator and consumer pump | Relatively low (heating) | Heat pump, condensing, underfloor heating |
| Injection circuit with PICV | constant flow | pressurised | variable | Constant / Variable | Generator and consumer pump | Relatively low (heating) | Air heaters |
| Mixing circuit | constant flow | unpressurised | variable | Constant / Variable | Consumer pump only | Low at low load (heating), high at low load (cooling) | Heating groups, air heaters at frost risk |
| Mixing circuit with fixed premix | constant flow | unpressurised | variable | Constant / Variable | Consumer pump only | Generally low | Underfloor/wall heating, concrete core |
| Diverting circuit | variable flow | pressurised | = generator | Variable / Constant | Generator pump | Heating: medium to high · cooling: medium to low | Reheaters, heat recovery, coolers with dehumidification |
| Injection circuit with three-way valve | constant flow | pressurised | variable | Constant / Constant | Generator and consumer pump | Relatively high (at 0 % load = generator flow temperature) | Air heaters with outdoor air |

*Sources: Siemens “Einführung in die HLK- und Gebäudetechnik” (08/2022), ch. 5.4–5.5, p. 144–153; Belimo selection table of hydraulic basic circuits (03/2023). Schematics without isolating and safety fittings; always check the design for the project.*

#### Valve kv/kvs & authority

*kv value, kvs selection, Δp, authority – also from power/ΔT* — `valve_kv`

```text
V̇ = P · 3600 / (ρ · c · ΔT)   (data of the valve list per VDI 3814 Part 4.1, Tab. 8: power, flow/return, ΔT)
kv = V̇ · √(ρ/1000 / Δp[bar])    Δp_V100 = (V̇ / kvs)² · ρ/1000 · 100 kPa
Valve authority a = Δp_V100 / (Δp_V100 + Δp_MV)
Δp_MV = pressure drop of the variable-flow section. Recommended a ≥ 0.5 (min. 0.3).
Water: ρ = 1000 kg/m³, c = 4.187 kJ/(kg·K). Glycol mixtures: take ρ and c from the manufacturer's data sheet.
Smallest controllable flow: V̇_min/V̇_100 = 1/√(1 + a·(S_v² − 1)), S_v = rangeability kvs/kvr; heat output with heat exchanger characteristic a_HX: Q̇/Q̇_100 = 1/(1 + a_HX·(V̇_100/V̇ − 1)). Example a = 0.5, S_v = 50: ≈ 3 % (no HX), ≈ 4 % (a_HX = 0.7). Two parallel valves 1/3 + 2/3 in sequence reduce the minimum output to about one third (QM Holzheizwerke, FAQ 37, 2015).
```

**Inputs:** Sizing from, Flow rate V̇ [m³/h], Power P [kW], Temperature difference ΔT (flow − return) [K], Medium, Density ρ [kg/m³], Specific heat c [kJ/(kg·K)], Desired Δp across valve [kPa], Δp variable-flow section [kPa], Rangeability S_v (kvs/kvr), Heat exchanger characteristic a_HX

**Results:** Flow rate V̇ [m³/h], Calculated kv [m³/h], Selected kvs (standard series) [m³/h], Δp at kvs (fully open) [kPa], Valve authority a, Smallest controllable flow [%], Smallest controllable heat output [%]

| DN 10 | 3/8" | 17.2 |

*Threaded pipes EN 10255 / ISO 65 or seamless/welded pipes EN 10220 (outer diameter in mm). The inch size is a nominal size, not the diameter. Wall thickness depends on the series (medium/heavy).*

## Surge & lightning protection

### Power supply 230/400 V

#### Surge protection 230/400 V: selection

*Type 1+2, 2 or 3 per location and protection zone, connection, U_C, I_imp/I_n, U_p* — `spd_power`

```text
Lightning protection zone concept (IEC 62305-4): LPZ 0A → 1 (external LPS or overhead line) lightning current arrester type 1 or combined arrester type 1+2; LPZ 0B → 1 and 1 → 2 surge arrester type 2; LPZ 2 → 3 type 3 at the equipment.
Next stage: type 2 in the sub-distribution if the cable to the upstream SPD is longer than 10 m; type 3 ahead of the equipment likewise (DEHN selection matrix).
I_imp per path (external LPS, IEC 62305-1 annex E simplified): 50 % of the lightning current of the LPL flows into the earth termination, 50 % divides over the n services and their m conductors: I_imp = 0.5 · I / (n · m). Without determination at least 12.5 kA per path (IEC 60364-5-53). The N–PE spark gap (3+1) carries the sum.
Type 2: I_n ≥ 5 kA per path (8/20 µs), commonly 20 kA. U_C ≥ 1.1 · U₀ = 253 V (L–N, L–PE), N–PE ≥ U₀; IT without N: L–PE ≥ U.
Protection level U_p ≤ 2.5 kV (overvoltage category II), effective protection up to about 10 m with U_p ≤ 0.8 · U_W = 2.0 kV.
Connection: a + b ≤ 0.5 m (V-wiring), Cu ≥ 16 mm² (type 1) or ≥ 6 mm² (type 2) to the equipotential bonding. In Germany only type 1 ahead of the meter (VDE-AR-N 4100). DEHN examples: DEHNventil, DEHNshield (type 1+2), DEHNguard (type 2), DEHNrail, DEHNflex, DEHNsafe (type 3). Check against the edition in force and manufacturer data.
```

**Inputs:** Location, Building / supply, Lightning protection level, Metallic services n, Cable length to the upstream SPD [m], Earthing system, Supply

**Results:** Arrester, Zone transition, Connection, U_C at least (L–N / L–PE) [V], U_C at least N–PE [V], I_imp per path (10/350 µs) [kA], I_imp total / N–PE [kA], I_n per path (8/20 µs) at least [kA], Protection level U_p at most [kV], Connection wiring, Back-up fuse, DEHN examples, Next protection stage

#### Effective protection level and protective distance

*U_p/f = U_p + ΔU, comparison with U_W, oscillation distance l_po* — `spd_distance`

```text
Effective protection level U_p/f = U_p + ΔU (connecting leads): for 230/400 V a flat ΔU = 0.5 kV (OVE information E 09) or about 1 kV per m of lead with lightning current (IEC 62305-4).
U_p/f ≤ 0.5 · U_W: protection independent of the cable length. Otherwise the conventional protective range of 10 m applies if U_p/f ≤ 0.8 · U_W; beyond that oscillation protective distance l_po = (U_W − U_p/f) / k with k = 25 V/m.
U_W = rated impulse withstand voltage of the equipment (230/400 V: category I 1.5 kV, II 2.5 kV, III 4 kV, IV 6 kV, IEC 60664-1). Check the induction protective distance for near/direct strikes per IEC 62305-4 annex as well.
```

**Inputs:** Protection level U_p of the SPD [kV], Lead allowance, Length of leads a + b [m], Equipment (overvoltage category), Cable length SPD → equipment [m]

**Results:** Effective protection level U_p/f [kV], Rated impulse withstand voltage U_W [kV], U_p/f / U_W [%], Oscillation protective distance l_po [m], Assessment

#### SPD connection per earthing system

| System | Connection | U_C L–N / L–PE | U_C N–PE |
|---|---|---|---|
| TN-C (3~) | 3+0 | ≥ 253 V | – |
| TN-S (3~) | 4+0 or 3+1 | ≥ 253 V | ≥ 230 V |
| TN-S (1~) | 2+0 or 1+1 | ≥ 253 V | ≥ 230 V |
| TT (3~) | 3+1 | ≥ 253 V | ≥ 230 V |
| TT (1~) | 1+1 | ≥ 253 V | ≥ 230 V |
| IT without N (3~) | 3+0 | L–PE ≥ U (400 V) | – |

*IEC 60364-5-53 (minimum U_C, table 534.2) and DEHN type designations. 3+1/1+1: varistors L–N and spark gap N–PE (CT2); usual in TT systems upstream of the RCD.*

#### Overvoltage categories and rated impulse voltage

| Category | U_W | Equipment |
|---|---|---|
| IV | 6 kV (4 kV) | at the origin: meter, main fuse, ripple control receiver |
| III | 4 kV (2,5 kV) | fixed installation: boards, switches, sockets, motors |
| II | 2,5 kV (1,5 kV) | appliances: household appliances, tools |
| I | 1,5 kV (0,8 kV) | particularly sensitive electronics |

*IEC 60664-1 / IEC 60364-4-44 table 443.2 for 230/400 V systems (120/208 V in brackets). The SPD must keep U_p/f below U_W of the equipment to be protected.*

| DEHNguard DG M TNS ACI 275 FM (952 440) | type 2 (+ type 3 coordinated ≤ 10 m) | TN-S 230/400 V | 275 V L–PE, 255 V N–PE | – | 20 kA | ≤ 1,5 kV | no external fuse (ACI); I_SCCR 25 kA | 4 mod., IP 20 |

### I&C, sensors & data lines

#### Surge protection I&C, sensors and data lines

*Arrester type by zone, U_C class, bandwidth, Pt100 error – with DEHN examples* — `spd_signal`

```text
Lines leaving the building (field devices outdoors, lines between buildings): zone transition LPZ 0A → 1, combined arrester with lightning current capability D1 (10/350 µs), e.g. 2.5 kA per core (DEHN BLITZDUCTOR XT ML4 BE 24: D1 2.5 kA per core, C2 10 kA per core). Inside the building (LPZ 1 → 2): surge arrester category C2 (8/20 µs).
U_C ≥ highest operating voltage of the signal incl. tolerance; rated current of the arrester ≥ signal/load current; cut-off frequency ≥ transmission frequency.
Pt100/Pt1000 in 2-wire connection: every core carries the series impedance R of the arrester, error ΔT = 2 · R · stages / (dR/dT); 3/4-wire compensates. In Ex i circuits only arresters approved for them.
LPL I (e.g. data centre) per VdS 2833: I_imp per core 2.5 kA with fewer than 3 pairs, 1 kA from 3, 0.5 kA from 10 pairs (DEHN WPX050 table 3).
Values per DEHN selection matrix industrial buildings (DS390) and BLITZDUCTOR data sheet; IEC 61643-21/-22, IEC 62305-4.
```

**Inputs:** Signal / interface, Cable route, Highest operating voltage of the signal [V], Sensor, Connection, Series impedance per core and arrester [Ω], Arresters in the loop, Pairs in the cable

**Results:** Arrester, Zone transition, U_C class at least [V], Transmission, DEHN examples, Installation, Error caused by the arrester (2-wire) [K], I_imp per core for LPL I (VdS 2833) [kA]

#### Test categories for data line arresters

| Category | Test voltage | Test current | Use |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | 0.5–1 kV (1.2/50 µs) | 0.25–0.5 kA (8/20 µs) | inductive coupling, fine protection |
| C2 | 2–10 kV (1.2/50 µs) | 1–5 kA (8/20 µs) | surge, LPZ 0B → 1 and higher |
| C3 | ≥ 1 kV (1 kV/µs) | 10–100 A (10/1000 µs) | protection level test (steep front) |
| D1 | ≥ 1 kV | 0.5–2.5 kA (10/350 µs) | partial lightning currents, LPZ 0A → 1 |

*IEC 61643-21 (selection). D1 = lightning current capability for LPZ 0A → 1, C2 = surge for LPZ 0B → 1 and higher. DEHN example BLITZDUCTOR XT ML4 BE 24: D1 2.5 kA per core (10 kA total), C2 10 kA per core. Check against the edition in force.*

| 3 | Server room | LPZ 1 → 2 (server LPZ 3) | type 2 in the floor box: DEHNguard ACI; large rooms additionally type 3 | BLITZDUCTORconnect BCO ML2 BD HF 5 | 952 440; 927 271 |
| 4 | Fire alarm system | LPZ 1 → 2 | type 3: DEHNrail DR M 2P 255 | BLITZDUCTORconnect BCO ML2 BE 24 | 953 205; 927 224 |
| 5 | Security centre, offices, IP cameras | LPZ 0B → 1, 1 → 2 | type 3: DEHNrail DR M 2P 255 | DEHNpatch DPA CL8 EA 4PPOE, DEHNpatch Outdoor DPA CLE IP66, BCO ML2 BD HF 5 | 953 205; 929 161; 929 221; 927 271 |
| 6 | PV system | LPZ 0B → 1 | type 2 PV: DEHNcube DCU YPV SCI 1000 2M | – | 900 920 |

*After DEHN WPX050 Bild 11 (data centre, LPL I). LV main: spark-gap based combined arrester with 100 kA (10/350 µs) total for LPL I; sub-distributions with type 2 because of cable lengths > 10 m (DEHNguard ACI without back-up fuse). Server room: unscreened type 2 in the floor box suffices, larger rooms additionally type 3 in the busbar tap-offs or the rack power supply; room = LPZ 2, server enclosure may be LPZ 3 (proven attenuation, bonding ≥ 6 mm² Cu). Ratings see tables "SPD for power" and "SPD for I&C and data". Examples – check against the current catalogue.*

#### Data centre: lightning protection, earthing, shielding

| Topic | Requirement | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| Protection level | usually LPL I (agree the risk assessment with the operator); impulse currents up to 200 kA (10/350 µs) | EN 62305-2, EN 50600 |
| Equipotential planes | reinforcement with 5 × 5 m meshes, bonded to the rebar every 1 m; shortens the separation distance and lowers the impedance | EN 62305-4 |
| Earthing | one common earthing system (lightning, MV, LV, telecom, shielding, bonding); ring earth with 5 × 5 m mesh below or outside the foundation earth, connected to it every 5 m | EN 62305-4, EN 50600-2-2 |
| Spatial shielding | a plain 5 × 5 m grid attenuates the lightning field by about a factor of 2 (6 dB); minimise openings, include windows and doors; assess against test level 3 (EN 61000-4-9/-10) | EN 62305-4 |
| Bonding network | meshed in three dimensions: pipes, rebar, gratings, cable and air ducts, lift rails, windows/doors; bond racks with short connections (preferably no round wire) | EN 62305-4, DIN VDE 0100-444 |
| Roof units | isolated air-termination (HVI conductor) keeps the separation distance; otherwise bond the lines via SPD: type 2 with I_n 20 kA per pole (LPL I), 15 kA (LPL II), 10 kA (LPL III/IV), data lines 10 kA per core | WPX050 table 1 |
| Protection zones | LV main supply LPZ 0A → 1 (type 1/combined arrester); sub-distributions type 2 (cables > 10 m: induction); server room LPZ 2, server enclosure LPZ 3 possible | EN 62305-4 |
| SPD connection | lead length ≤ 0.5 m; protect every infeed of coupled transformers alike; type 2 also in the outgoing feeders of large LV main boards | IEC 60364-5-53 |
| Server room | unscreened: type 2 in the floor box suffices; larger rooms additionally type 3 in the busbar tap-offs or the rack power supply; server enclosure as LPZ 3 only with proven attenuation, bonding ≥ 6 mm² Cu | WPX050 |

*Design principles from DEHN WPX050 "Lightning and surge protection concept for data centres" (04/2022) with EN 62305-2/-3/-4, EN 50600-1/-2-2, IEC 60364-5-53, IEC 60364-4-44. Check against the edition in force.*

#### Surge protection in hazardous areas (intrinsically safe loop)

*Arresters for Ex i loops: type of protection, U_i/I_i against U_o/I_o, floating circuits, installation – after DEHN WPX035* — `spd_ex`

```text
After DEHN WPX035 "Lightning and surge protection for hazardous areas" and IEC 60079-11/-14/-25, IEC 62305-4:
• Location: transition LPZ 0B → 1 at the field device (e.g. temperature transmitter on the tank) and at the other end of the field cable with the same discharge capacity; after the isolating amplifier in the control room (LPZ 2) no further SPD is needed if the screen is bonded at both ends.
• Discharge capacity at least 10 impulses of 10 kA (8/20 µs) without defect; arrester in a metal-shielded enclosure, earthing at least 4 mm² Cu; line arrester → equipment in a metal conduit earthed at both ends or shielded and at most 1 m long.
• Type of protection: zone 0/20 ia, zone 1/21 ia or ib, zone 2/22 ia, ib or ic; an arrester certified ia also suits ib/ic.
• Interconnection: U_i (or U_C) of the arrester ≥ U_o of the supply, I_i (rated current) ≥ I_o; include internal capacitance C_i and inductance L_i in the intrinsic safety verification.
• Floating: transmitters with insulation ≥ 500 V AC count as floating – the arrester must not cancel this; below 500 V AC the loop counts as earthed, then protection level U_p (core–PG) at 10 kA (8/20 µs) below the insulation (e.g. ≤ 35 V).
• Equipment per NAMUR NE 21: 0.5 kV core/core, 1 kV core/earth (IEC 61000-4-5).
Defaults = example table 2 (transmitter U_o 29.4 V, I_o 130 mA; BLITZDUCTOR XT ML4 BD EX 24: ia, U_i 30 V, I_i 500 mA, 20 kA, U_p ≤ 52 V). Check product data against the current certificate.
```

**Inputs:** Hazardous zone at the field device, U_o of the supply / loop [V], I_o (short-circuit current on fault) [mA], Type of protection of the arrester, U_i or U_C of the arrester [V], I_i or rated current of the arrester [mA], Insulation transmitter to earth, Line arrester → field device [m], Routing of this line

**Results:** Required type of protection, U_i of the arrester at least [V], I_i of the arrester at least [mA], Location, Discharge capacity, Protection level / floating, Line to the field device, Earthing and screen, DEHN examples

#### Hazardous areas: lightning and surge protection

| Topic | Requirement | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| Protection level | at least II (rolling sphere 30 m, mesh 10 × 10 m); deviation only if justified or per risk assessment | DIN EN 62305-3 |
| Air-termination | install outside the Ex zones (sparks at the point of strike); natural parts (roof, pipes, vessels) only from 5 mm steel and without dangerous heating | IEC 62305-3 annex D |
| Down conductors | one per 10 m of roof perimeter, at least 2; bonding at ground level and every 20 m of height; continuous rebar permitted | DIN EN 62305-3 |
| Separation distance | calculate at the point of proximity (lightning current divided over the down conductors) | DIN EN 62305-3 |
| Building shielding | connect metal façade and rebar into a screening cage: magnetic field 10 to 300 times smaller | DIN EN 62305-4 |
| SPD location | transition LPZ 0B → 1, before surges reach Ex zone 0/20; same discharge capacity at the other end of the field cable | DIN EN 62305-4 |
| SPD discharge capacity | at least 10 impulses of 10 kA (8/20 µs) without defect | DIN EN 60079-11/-14/-25 |
| SPD enclosure | metal-shielded enclosure, earthing ≥ 4 mm² Cu | DIN EN 60079-14 |
| Line SPD → device | in a metal conduit earthed at both ends or shielded, at most 1 m | DIN EN 60079-14 |
| Floating circuits | transmitter ≥ 500 V AC to earth = floating, the SPD must not cancel this; < 500 V AC = earthed: U_p (core–PG) at 10 kA below the insulation, e.g. ≤ 35 V | DIN EN 60079-11 |
| Type of protection | SPD certified ia also suits ib/ic; U_i ≥ U_o and I_i ≥ I_o of the supply; include C_i/L_i in the verification | DIN EN 60079-11/-25 |
| Immunity | NAMUR NE 21: 0.5 kV core/core, 1 kV core/earth (IEC 61000-4-5); test level 1–4 on the protective devices (e.g. P1) | NE 21, IEC 61000-4-5 |
| Earth termination | own earthing per building/plant section, all meshed; mesh 20 × 20 m, 10 × 10 m in Ex areas and with electronics | DIN 18014, DIN EN 62305, DIN VDE 0151 |
| Equipotential bonding | consistently including columns, pipes, vessels; secure connections against loosening; supplementary bonding per IEC 60079-14 (IEC 60364-4-41/-5-54); earth conductor at the SPD ≥ 4 mm² Cu | DIN EN 60079-14 |
| Cable screen | earth at both ends if the earth termination is meshed (10 × 10 m) and an insulated bonding conductor ≥ 4 mm² (better 16 mm²) runs parallel to the line to the same bonding bar | DIN EN 60079-14 |
| Control room (LPZ 1 → 2) | shielded line bonded at both ends: no further SPD needed at the transition LPZ 1 → 2 | WPX035 |

*From DEHN WPX035 "Lightning and surge protection for hazardous areas" (01/2022) with IEC 62305-3/-4, IEC 60079-11/-14/-25, NAMUR NE 21, DIN 18014, DIN VDE 0151. Select arresters with the calculator "Surge protection in hazardous areas". Check against the edition in force.*

### Protection zones & risk

#### Lightning protection zones (LPZ) and arresters

| Zone | Threat | Transition | Power | IT / I&C |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| LPZ 0A | direct strike, full lightning field | 0A → 1 | type 1 or combined type 1+2 | combined arrester, D1 (10/350 µs) |
| LPZ 0B | no direct strike, full field | 0B → 1 | type 2 | surge arrester C2 |
| LPZ 1 | partial currents and surges limited | 1 → 2 | type 2 | surge arrester C2 |
| LPZ 2 | further attenuated (screened rooms, cabinets) | 2 → 3 | type 3 | fine protection C1/C2 |
| LPZ 3 | device/module | – | – | – |

*IEC 62305-4, presentation after the DEHN lightning protection guide. At every zone boundary all lines are bonded – directly or via SPD.*

#### SPD types for power

| Type | Test | Use | Typical values | DEHN examples |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| type 1 (lightning current arrester) | I_imp 10/350 µs | origin with LPS/overhead line (LPZ 0A → 1) | 12.5–25 kA per path, U_p ≤ 1.5–2.5 kV | DEHNbloc, DEHNgap, DEHNvenCI |
| type 1+2 (combined arrester) | I_imp and I_n | main distribution, meter cabinet | U_p ≤ 1.5 kV, I_imp 12.5–25 kA per path | DEHNventil, DEHNshield |
| type 2 (surge arrester) | I_n 8/20 µs | main and sub-distribution (LPZ 0B → 1, 1 → 2) | I_n 20 kA, I_max 40 kA, U_p ≤ 1.5 kV | DEHNguard M/MP/S |
| type 3 (device protection) | combination wave 1.2/50 µs, 8/20 µs | ahead of the equipment (LPZ 2 → 3) | U_OC 6 kV, I_L 16–25 A | DEHNrail, DEHNflex, DEHNsafe, DEHNprotector |

*IEC 61643-11 (type = class I/II/III or T1/T2/T3). Energy coordination of the stages per manufacturer; DEHN examples (short catalogue 08/2023).*

#### Discharge capacity by protection level

| Application | Connection / line | Discharge capacity | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Power, LPL I | 4+0: L–PE and N–PE | 25 kA each (10/350 µs) | VdS 2833 (WPX050 tab. 2) |
| Power, LPL I | 3+1: L–N / N–PE | 25 kA / 100 kA (10/350 µs) | VdS 2833 (WPX050 tab. 2) |
| Data lines, LPL I | < 3 pairs | 2.5 kA per core (10/350 µs) | VdS 2833 (WPX050 tab. 3) |
| Data lines, LPL I | ≥ 3 pairs | 1 kA per core | VdS 2833 (WPX050 tab. 3) |
| Data lines, LPL I | ≥ 10 pairs | 0.5 kA per core | VdS 2833 (WPX050 tab. 3) |
| Type 2 without separation distance, LPL I | power / data | I_n 20 kA per pole / 10 kA per core (8/20 µs) | WPX050 tab. 1 |
| Type 2 without separation distance, LPL II | power / data | I_n 15 kA per pole / 10 kA per core | WPX050 tab. 1 |
| Type 2 without separation distance, LPL III/IV | power / data | I_n 10 kA per pole / 10 kA per core | WPX050 tab. 1 |
| Hazardous area, intrinsically safe loop | every core | 10 impulses of 10 kA (8/20 µs) without defect | IEC 60079-14 (WPX035) |

*After DEHN WPX050 tables 1–3 (VdS 2833) and WPX035. LPL I = highest protection level (data centres). For other levels determine I_imp with the calculator "Surge protection 230/400 V" (current split). Check against the edition in force.*

#### Lightning protection levels: current and air-termination

| Level | I_max (10/350 µs) | Charge Q_short | W/R | I_min | Rolling sphere | Mesh size | Down conductor spacing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| I | 200 kA | 100 C | 10 MJ/Ω | 3 kA | 20 m | 5 × 5 m | 10 m |
| II | 150 kA | 75 C | 5,6 MJ/Ω | 5 kA | 30 m | 10 × 10 m | 10 m |
| III | 100 kA | 50 C | 2,5 MJ/Ω | 10 kA | 45 m | 15 × 15 m | 15 m |
| IV | 100 kA | 50 C | 2,5 MJ/Ω | 16 kA | 60 m | 20 × 20 m | 20 m |

*IEC 62305-1 tables 3/4 and IEC 62305-3 tables 2/4, typical spacing of down conductors per DEHN WPX035 table 1 (in Ex areas additionally one down conductor per 10 m of perimeter, at least 2). Maximum values size the arresters, minimum values the air-termination radius. Check against the edition in force.*

#### Induced voltage in installation loops

*U = M · Δi/Δt of a conductor loop next to a down conductor (LEMP) – DEHN WPX050 example: 720 kV* — `lemp_loop`

```text
Induced square-wave voltage in an open or closed conductor loop (e.g. alarm or supply line) next to a down conductor carrying lightning current: U = M · Δi/Δt (DEHN WPX050 Bild 14/15).
M = mutual inductance of the loop (from the DEHN chart for square loops a × a at distance s; example a = 10 m, s = 3 m → M₂ ≈ 4.8 µH). Δi/Δt = steepness of the lightning current per IEC 62305-1 table 3: first stroke 20/15/10 kA/µs, subsequent stroke 200/150/100 kA/µs for LPL I/II/III–IV.
WPX050 example: 4.8 µH · 150 kA/µs = 720 kV. Remedy: keep loops small (route go and return together), distance to down conductors, shielding (bonded at both ends) and SPD at the loop ends.
```

**Inputs:** Mutual inductance M of the loop [µH], Lightning protection level, Lightning current component, Own steepness Δi/Δt [kA/µs]

**Results:** Induced voltage U [kV], Steepness Δi/Δt [kA/µs]

#### Risk assessment for overvoltages of atmospheric origin

*Assessment per IEC 60364-4-44, 443.5 (CRL)* — `surge_risk`

```text
IEC 60364-4-44:2007+A1:2015 section 443:
Protection against transient overvoltages is always required where overvoltages affect human life (e.g. safety services, medical), public services and cultural heritage, commercial/industrial activity or a large number of persons (443.4). Otherwise risk assessment:
L_P = 2 · L_PAL + L_PCL + 0.4 · L_PAH + 0.2 · L_PCH (km; LV overhead, LV cable, HV overhead, HV cable from the building, together at most 1 km)    CRL = f_env / (L_P · N_g)
f_env = 85 (rural/suburban), 850 (urban). CRL < 1000 → overvoltage protection required.
N_g = lightning ground flash density (flashes per km² and year) from flash density maps.
Check against the edition in force.
```

**Inputs:** Consequences for life, public services, business or many persons?, Environment, Flash density N_g [1/(km²·a)], LV overhead line L_PAL [km], LV cable L_PCL [km], HV overhead line L_PAH [km], HV cable L_PCH [km]

**Results:** Result, Risk assessment length L_P [km], Calculated risk level CRL

## Explosion protection (ATEX)

### Explosion protection: temperature class & marking

*Zone → category/EPL, substance → T class & group, dust → T_max, equipment check* — `ex_selection`

```text
Gas/vapour: the maximum surface temperature of the equipment must stay below the ignition temperature.
Temperature classes (IEC 60079-0): T1 450 °C · T2 300 °C · T3 200 °C · T4 135 °C · T5 100 °C · T6 85 °C.
Gas group (IEC 60079-20-1): IIA ⊂ IIB ⊂ IIC – IIC equipment may also be used for IIB/IIA.
Dust (IEC 60079-14): T_max ≤ 2/3 · T_cloud and T_max ≤ T_5mm − 75 K (layers up to 5 mm).
Zone 0/1/2 → category 1G/2G/3G or EPL Ga/Gb/Gc; zone 20/21/22 → 1D/2D/3D or Da/Db/Dc (ATEX 2014/34/EU, 1999/92/EC). Substance data are guide values (Schmersal/Festo documents, GESTIS) – the safety data sheet, IEC 60079-20-1 and the explosion protection document are decisive.
```

**Inputs:** Zone, Substance (gas/vapour), Ignition temperature (own substance) [°C], Gas group (own substance), Dust group, Ignition temperature dust cloud [°C], Ignition temperature 5 mm layer [°C], Dust layer thickness [mm], Equipment: temperature class, Equipment: gas group, Equipment: max. surface temperature [°C]

**Results:** Equipment category (EPL), Occurrence of explosive atmosphere, Ignition temperature [°C], Required temperature class, Permissible surface temperature [°C], Required group, Marking (example), Suitable types of protection

### Ex zones, categories, EPL

| Zone | Atmosphere | Occurrence | Category | EPL | Types of protection |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Zone 0 | Gas/vapour | continuously, for long periods or frequently | 1G | Ga | ia, ma (or two independent Gb types) |
| Zone 1 | Gas/vapour | occasionally in normal operation | 2G | Gb | db, eb, ib, mb, pxb, pyb, ob, qb – and all Ga equipment |
| Zone 2 | Gas/vapour | not in normal operation, if so only briefly | 3G | Gc | ec (formerly nA), ic, mc, pzc, oc, nR – and all Ga/Gb equipment |
| Zone 20 | Dust | dust cloud continuously, long-term or frequently | 1D | Da | ta, ia, ma |
| Zone 21 | Dust | dust cloud occasionally in normal operation | 2D | Db | tb, ib, mb, pb – and all Da equipment |
| Zone 22 | Dust | dust cloud not in normal operation, if so only briefly | 3D | Dc | tc, ic, mc, pc – and all Da/Db equipment |

*Sources: IEC/EN 60079-0, -14, -20-1, ATEX 2014/34/EU and 1999/92/EC, manufacturer documents (Schmersal, Festo). Check against the edition in force and the explosion protection document.*

### Ex groups (gas and dust)

| Group | Area | MESG | MIC ratio | Example |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| I | Mining (firedamp) | – | – | Methane |
| IIA | Gas – other areas | > 0,9 mm | > 0,8 | Propane |
| IIB | Gas | 0,5 … 0,9 mm | 0,45 … 0,8 | Ethylene |
| IIC | Gas | < 0,5 mm | < 0,45 | Hydrogen, acetylene |
| IIIA | Dust | – | – | combustible flyings |
| IIIB | Dust | – | – | non-conductive dust (wood, flour) |
| IIIC | Dust | – | – | conductive dust (metal, carbon) |

*Sources: IEC/EN 60079-0, -14, -20-1, ATEX 2014/34/EU and 1999/92/EC, manufacturer documents (Schmersal, Festo). Check against the edition in force and the explosion protection document.*

### Ex temperature classes T1–T6

| Class | Max. surface temperature | Ignition temperature of substance | Examples |
|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | 450 °C | > 450 °C | Methane, hydrogen, ammonia, propane, acetone |
| T2 | 300 °C | > 300 … 450 °C | Ethylene, ethanol, methanol, n-butane, acetylene |
| T3 | 200 °C | > 200 … 300 °C | Diesel, cyclohexane, hydrogen sulphide |
| T4 | 135 °C | > 135 … 200 °C | Diethyl ether, acetaldehyde |
| T5 | 100 °C | > 100 … 135 °C | (rare) |
| T6 | 85 °C | > 85 … 100 °C | Carbon disulphide |

*Sources: IEC/EN 60079-0, -14, -20-1, ATEX 2014/34/EU and 1999/92/EC, manufacturer documents (Schmersal, Festo). Check against the edition in force and the explosion protection document.*

### Types of protection

| Code | Type of protection | Principle | Level → zone | Standard |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| d | Flameproof enclosure | Explosion contained in enclosure | db → 1, dc → 2 | IEC 60079-1 |
| e | Increased safety | no sparks/hot spots | eb → 1, ec → 2 | IEC 60079-7 |
| i | Intrinsic safety | energy limited | ia → 0, ib → 1, ic → 2 | IEC 60079-11 |
| p | Pressurized enclosure | purge gas overpressure | pxb/pyb → 1, pzc → 2 | IEC 60079-2 |
| m | Encapsulation | embedded in compound | ma → 0, mb → 1, mc → 2 | IEC 60079-18 |
| o | Liquid immersion | under protective liquid | ob → 1, oc → 2 | IEC 60079-6 |
| q | Powder filling | filled with sand | qb → 1 | IEC 60079-5 |
| nR | Restricted breathing | tight enclosure | nR → 2 | IEC 60079-15 |
| t | Protection by enclosure (dust) | dust-tight, limited temperature | ta → 20, tb → 21, tc → 22 | IEC 60079-31 |
| op | Optical radiation | is/pr/sh | Ga/Gb/Gc | IEC 60079-28 |

*Sources: IEC/EN 60079-0, -14, -20-1, ATEX 2014/34/EU and 1999/92/EC, manufacturer documents (Schmersal, Festo). Check against the edition in force and the explosion protection document.*

| Hydrogen | 560 °C | T1 | IIC |

## P&ID tagging

### P&ID tag finder

*Enter a name (e.g. supply air temperature, methane, leakage) → ISA, EN 62424, VDI 3814* — `pid_tags`

```text
First letter = measured variable (T temperature, P pressure, F flow, L level, M moisture, A/Q analysis …), succeeding letters = function: E element, T transmitter, I indicate, C control, S switch, A alarm, H/L high/low, Q totalize. Tag = letters + loop number, e.g. TT-101.
For BACS also the component code and user address per VDI 3814-4.1 (code + no. _ function _ no.).
Letters per ANSI/ISA-5.1-2009 and EN 62424 (successor of DIN 19227-1); BACS codes per VDI 3814 Part 4.1 Tab. 3/4 (examples), classes per IEC 81346-2 as listed in VDI 3814-4.1. Project conventions take precedence.
```

**Inputs:** Measuring point / device (name), Function, Location / symbol, Loop number

**Results:** Recognised, Letters ISA-5.1, Example tag (ISA), Meaning, Letters EN 62424 / DIN 19227, BACS code VDI 3814-4.1, User address (component_function), Class IEC 81346-2, Signal, P&ID symbol, Other matches

| Plant switch (hand-off-auto) | HVAC | HS | HS | ANL | SF |
| Maintenance (repair) switch | HVAC | HS | HS | – | SF |

*Typical function per device; choose another function (element, indicator, controller, high/low) in the tag finder.
Letters per ANSI/ISA-5.1-2009 and EN 62424 (successor of DIN 19227-1); BACS codes per VDI 3814 Part 4.1 Tab. 3/4 (examples), classes per IEC 81346-2 as listed in VDI 3814-4.1. Project conventions take precedence.*

| Letter | Measured variable (1st letter) | Modifier | Readout/passive function | Output function | Function modifier |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | Analysis | – | Alarm | – | – |
| B | Burner, combustion | – | user | user | user |

| K | Time, schedule | Rate of change | – | Control station | – |
| L | Level | – | Light | – | Low |
| M | user (often moisture) | – | – | – | Middle |
| N | user | – | user | user | user |
| O | user | – | Orifice, restriction | – | Open |
| P | Pressure | – | Test point | – | – |
| Q | Quantity | Integrate, totalize | Integrate, totalize | – | – |

*ANSI/ISA-5.1-2009 Tab. 4.1 (paraphrased). “user” = user's choice. Example: TIC-101 = temperature, indicate, control, loop 101.*

| Letter | As first letter | As modifier/succeeding letter |
|---|---|---|
| A | – | Fault message (alarm) |
| C | – | Automatic control |
| D | Density | Difference |

| F | Flow | Ratio |

| H | Manual input | Upper limit |
| I | – | Indication |

## Cleanroom & pharma

### Cleanroom class ISO 14644-1

*Class limit ↔ class from measurement* — `iso14644_class`

```text
C_n = 10^N · (0.1 / D)^2.08   [particles/m³]   (ISO 14644-1:2015, formula A.1)
N = log₁₀(C_n) + 2.08 · log₁₀(D / 0.1)
Table values are rounded to whole numbers with at most 3 significant figures. Intermediate classes in 0.1 steps are allowed. For ≥ 5 µm at ISO 5 and cleaner use the macroparticle descriptor (M).
```

**Inputs:** Given, ISO class N, Measured concentration [1/m³], Particle size

**Results:** Max. concentration (table) [1/m³], Max. concentration [1/ft³], Max. concentration [1/l], Class number (exact), ISO class

### Sampling locations & volume

*Classification sampling per ISO 14644-1* — `iso14644_sampling`

```text
Minimum sampling locations N_L per ISO 14644-1:2015 Table A.1 (area in m²); above 1000 m²: N_L = 27 · A / 1000.
Minimum single sample volume: V_s = 20 / C_n,m · 1000 [l], C_n,m = class limit of the largest considered particle size. At least 2 l and at least 1 min sampling time.
```

**Inputs:** Cleanroom area [m²], ISO class, Largest considered particle size, Particle counter flow rate [l/min]

**Results:** Minimum sampling locations, Minimum sample volume per location [l], Minimum sampling time per location [min], Total sampling time (excl. moving) [min]

### Air change & recovery time

*ACH, airflow, 100:1 recovery time* — `air_change`

```text
n = V̇ / V_room [1/h]    Recovery with ideal mixing: t = ln(k) / n
100:1 (recovery test ISO 14644-3): t = 4.61 / n [h] = 276 / n [min]. Real rooms have a lower ventilation effectiveness ε – measured values are usually longer. EU GMP Annex 1: recovery to "at rest" typically ≤ 15–20 min.
```

**Inputs:** Room area [m²], Room height [m], Given, Supply airflow [m³/h], Air change rate [1/h], Ventilation effectiveness ε

**Results:** Air change rate [1/h], Supply airflow [m³/h], Room volume [m³], Recovery time 100:1 [min], Recovery time 10:1 [min]

### FFU ceiling (fan filter units)

*FFU count, airflow, power, energy cost* — `ffu_ceiling`

```text
n_FFU = ⌈A_room · coverage / A_FFU⌉    V̇ = n_FFU · A_FFU · v · 3600
Air change = V̇ / (A · H)    P_el = n_FFU · P_FFU (all of it ends up as heat in the room!)
Guide value unidirectional flow (grade A/ISO 5): v ≈ 0.36–0.54 m/s (former EU GMP guidance 0.45 m/s ± 20 %).
```

**Inputs:** Cleanroom area [m²], Room height [m], Ceiling coverage [%], FFU size (nominal), Face velocity [m/s], Power per FFU [W], Operating hours per year [h], Energy price per kWh [€ / CHF]

**Results:** Number of FFUs, Total airflow [m³/h], Air change rate [1/h], Electrical power / heat load [kW], Energy per year [MWh], Energy cost per year [€]

### Overflow / pressure cascade

*Leakage air through door gaps at Δp* — `pressure_cascade`

```text
Q = C_d · A · √(2 · Δp / ρ)    (orifice equation, C_d ≈ 0.61 for sharp-edged gaps)
A = gap length · gap width + other openings. The result is the overflow air the HVAC must supply in addition to keep Δp.
EU GMP Annex 1: pressure differential between adjacent rooms of different grades typ. 10–15 Pa (guidance).
```

**Inputs:** Total gap length (e.g. door perimeter) [m], Gap width [mm], Other openings [cm²], Pressure differential Δp [Pa], Discharge coefficient C_d

**Results:** Leakage area [cm²], Overflow airflow [m³/h], Velocity in gap [m/s]

### ISO 14644-1: class limits

| Class | 0,1 µm | 0,2 µm | 0,3 µm | 0,5 µm | 1 µm | 5 µm |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ISO 1 | 10 | 2 | – | – | – | – |
| ISO 2 | 100 | 24 | 10 | 4 | – | – |
| ISO 3 | 1 000 | 237 | 102 | 35 | 8 | – |
| ISO 4 | 10 000 | 2 370 | 1 020 | 352 | 83 | – |
| ISO 5 | 100 000 | 23 700 | 10 200 | 3 520 | 832 | – |
| ISO 6 | 1 000 000 | 237 000 | 102 000 | 35 200 | 8 320 | 293 |
| ISO 7 | – | – | – | 352 000 | 83 200 | 2 930 |
| ISO 8 | – | – | – | 3 520 000 | 832 000 | 29 300 |
| ISO 9 | – | – | – | 35 200 000 | 8 320 000 | 293 000 |

*ISO 14644-1:2015 Table 1, calculated with C_n = 10^N · (0.1/D)^2.08 and rounded. "–" = not applicable.*

### EU GMP Annex 1: particle limits

| Grade | At rest ≥ 0.5 µm | At rest ≥ 5 µm | In operation ≥ 0.5 µm | In operation ≥ 5 µm |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | 3 520 | n/s | 3 520 | 29 |
| B | 3 520 | n/s | 352 000 | 2 930 |
| C | 352 000 | 2 930 | 3 520 000 | 29 300 |
| D | 3 520 000 | 29 300 | n/s | n/s |

*EU GMP Guide Annex 1 (2022), Table 1/5 – max. permitted particles/m³. n/s = not specified. ISO equivalent: A ≈ ISO 5, B ≈ ISO 5 (at rest) / ISO 7 (in operation), C ≈ ISO 7 / ISO 8, D ≈ ISO 8 (at rest). Check against the version in force.*

### EU GMP Annex 1: microbial limits

| Grade | Air CFU/m³ | Settle plates CFU/4 h | Contact CFU/plate | Glove CFU/glove |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | no growth | no growth | no growth | no growth |
| B | 10 | 5 | 5 | 5 |
| C | 100 | 50 | 25 | – |
| D | 200 | 100 | 50 | – |

*EU GMP Guide Annex 1 (2022), Table 6 – action limits in operation. Grade A: no growth expected.*

## Data centre

### PUE / energy efficiency

*PUE from power or annual energy, DCiE, cost, EnEfG* — `dc_pue`

```text
PUE = E_total / E_IT (ISO/IEC 30134-2: annual energy, metered at the utility point and at the UPS/PDU output)
From power (instantaneous): PUE = P_total / P_IT, annual energy = P · 8760 h    DCiE = 1 / PUE · 100 %
Overhead = total − IT (cooling, UPS losses, lighting …)
German EnEfG (2023) §11: new DCs starting operation from 1 July 2026 PUE ≤ 1.2; existing DCs PUE ≤ 1.5 from 1 July 2027 and ≤ 1.3 from 1 July 2030; new DCs ERF ≥ 10 % (from July 2026), 15 % (2027), 20 % (2028). Cabinet draft of the amendment of 24 June 2026: existing 1.6 / 1.4, new 1.2 (4-year period), scope from 500 kW IT load – not yet passed (as of July 2026). Check the version in force.
```

**Inputs:** Basis, Total facility power [kW], IT power [kW], Total facility energy per year [MWh], IT energy per year [MWh], Energy price per kWh [€ / CHF], Data centre, EnEfG version (Germany only)

**Results:** PUE, DCiE [%], Overhead power (average) [kW], Total energy per year [MWh], Overhead cost per year [€], Saving at PUE 1.3 [MWh/a]

### PUE balance from consumers

*PUE, pPUE, annual energy, cost, CO₂, waste heat – from IT, UPS, cooling* — `dc_pue_balance`

```text
UPS loss = P_IT · (1/η_UPS − 1)    distribution loss = (P_IT + UPS loss) · v (transformer, cabling before the UPS)
Cooling load Q = P_IT + UPS loss + other    P_cooling = Q · (1 − free cooling share) / EER + Q · auxiliaries
P_total = P_IT + UPS + distribution losses + P_cooling + other    PUE = P_total / P_IT
pPUE cooling = (P_IT + P_cooling) / P_IT    pPUE power = (P_IT + losses) / P_IT (ISO/IEC 30134-2 annex)
ERF = reused heat / total energy (ISO/IEC 30134-6). Annual values with 8760 h and average powers.
Take annual EER and free cooling share (climate, chilled water temperature) from the designer/manufacturer.
German EnEfG (2023) §11: new DCs starting operation from 1 July 2026 PUE ≤ 1.2; existing DCs PUE ≤ 1.5 from 1 July 2027 and ≤ 1.3 from 1 July 2030; new DCs ERF ≥ 10 % (from July 2026), 15 % (2027), 20 % (2028). Cabinet draft of the amendment of 24 June 2026: existing 1.6 / 1.4, new 1.2 (4-year period), scope from 500 kW IT load – not yet passed (as of July 2026). Check the version in force.
```

**Inputs:** IT power (annual average) [kW], UPS efficiency [%], Transformer/distribution losses [%], Lighting, security, other [kW], Chiller EER (annual average), Free cooling share [%], Fans/pumps/dry coolers (relative to cooling load) [%], Energy price per kWh [€ / CHF], CO₂ emission factor [kg/kWh], Reused waste heat (average) [kW], Data centre, EnEfG version (Germany only)

**Results:** PUE, Total facility power (average) [kW], pPUE cooling, pPUE power supply, DCiE [%], UPS losses [kW], Distribution losses [kW], Cooling load [kW], Cooling power [kW], Cooling share of total [%], Total annual energy [MWh], IT annual energy [MWh], Energy cost per year [€], of which overhead (non-IT) [€], CO₂ per year [t], Waste heat available (cooling load) [MWh/a], ERF (energy reuse factor) [%]

### Rack cooling & redundancy

*Airflow per rack, cooling units N+1/2N* — `dc_rack_cooling`

```text
All IT power becomes heat.  V̇ = P / (ρ · c · ΔT)   (air: ρ = 1.2 kg/m³, c = 1.005 kJ/(kg·K))
ΔT = server exhaust − inlet temperature (typ. 10–15 K). Number of cooling units: N = ⌈load / unit capacity⌉ plus redundancy. Recommended inlet temperature per ASHRAE TC 9.9: 18–27 °C.
```

**Inputs:** Number of racks, Power per rack [kW], ΔT across servers [K], Capacity per cooling unit (CRAH/CRAC) [kW], Redundancy

**Results:** Total IT heat load [kW], Airflow per rack [m³/h], Airflow per rack [CFM], Total airflow [m³/h], Cooling units incl. redundancy, Installed cooling capacity [kW]

### UPS sizing

*Modules with redundancy, loading, battery autonomy* — `dc_ups`

```text
P_design = (P_IT + P_other) · (1 + reserve)    S = P / cos φ
Modules N = ⌈P / (P_module · max. loading)⌉ plus redundancy (N+1; 2N = two separate systems).
Battery: E = P · t / η_inv    I_DC = P / (η_inv · U_DC). For short autonomy select batteries from the manufacturer's constant-power table (W/cell) – the nominal Ah rating will be much higher than E / U.
```

**Inputs:** IT load [kW], Other protected load [kW], Growth reserve [%], Load power factor, Module rating (active power) [kW], Max. loading per module [%], Redundancy, Autonomy time [min], Battery nominal voltage [V], Inverter efficiency [%]

**Results:** Design power [kW], Apparent power [kVA], Modules without redundancy (N), Total modules incl. redundancy, Loading per module (normal) [%], Battery energy (discharge) [kWh], Battery discharge current [A]

### Availability & downtime

*Percent ↔ downtime per year, tier comparison* — `dc_availability`

```text
Downtime = (1 − A) · 8760 h    Series: A = A1 · A2    Parallel (redundant): A = 1 − (1 − A1)·(1 − A2)
Frequently quoted guide values: Tier I 99.671 %, Tier II 99.741 %, Tier III 99.982 %, Tier IV 99.995 % (not official Uptime Institute criteria).
```

**Inputs:** Availability of one component/path [%], Number of parallel (redundant) paths

**Results:** Total availability [%], Downtime per year [h], Downtime per year [min], Downtime per month [min]

### ASHRAE TC 9.9: IT environmental classes

| Range | Temperature | Humidity | Max. dew point |
|---|---|---|---|
| Recommended (A1–A4) | 18–27 °C | DP −9 °C … 15 °C, ≤ 60 % RH | 15 °C |
| Allowable A1 | 15–32 °C | 8–80 % RH | 17 °C |
| Allowable A2 | 10–35 °C | 8–80 % RH | 21 °C |
| Allowable A3 | 5–40 °C | 8–85 % RH | 24 °C |
| Allowable A4 | 5–45 °C | 8–90 % RH | 24 °C |

*ASHRAE Thermal Guidelines for Data Processing Environments (4th/5th ed.). Values at the IT equipment air inlet; check the edition in force and vendor data.*

### Availability classes EN 50600 / tier

| EN 50600 | Compare | Design | Property |
|---|---|---|---|
| Class 1 | Tier I | single path, no redundancy | low availability |
| Class 2 | Tier II | single path, redundant components (N+1) | medium availability |
| Class 3 | Tier III | multiple paths, one active | concurrently maintainable |
| Class 4 | Tier IV | multiple active paths (2N) | fault tolerant |

*EN 50600-1 availability classes 1–4; Uptime Institute Tier I–IV for comparison (not identical).*

## PV & storage

### PV string design

*Modules per string for the inverter, string fuses* — `pv_string`

```text
X(T) = X_STC · (1 + β · (T − 25 °C))   for V_oc and V_mpp (β negative, datasheet)
n_max = min(⌊V_DC,max / V_oc(T_min)⌋, ⌊V_MPP,max / V_mpp(T_min)⌋)    n_min = ⌈V_MPP,min / V_mpp(T_max)⌉
T_min = lowest cell temperature (DE typ. −10 °C, alpine/CH down to −20 °C), T_max = summer cell temperature (typ. 70 °C).
String fuses required if (N_p − 1) · 1.25 · I_sc > module reverse current rating (IEC 62548); rating then 1.5 · I_sc < I_n ≤ 2.4 · I_sc and ≤ reverse current rating.
```

**Inputs:** Module V_oc (STC) [V], Module V_mpp (STC) [V], Module I_sc (STC) [A], Module power [Wp], Temp. coefficient V_oc [%/K], Temp. coefficient V_mpp (≈ P_max) [%/K], Min. cell temperature [°C], Max. cell temperature [°C], Inverter max. DC input voltage [V], Inverter MPP range min. [V], Inverter MPP range max. [V], Inverter max. I_SC per MPPT [A], Parallel strings per MPPT, Module reverse current rating [A], Planned modules per string

**Results:** V_oc per module at T_min [V], V_mpp per module at T_max [V], Modules per string, String V_oc at T_min (planned) [V], String V_mpp at T_max (planned) [V], Power per string (planned) [kWp], Current per MPPT (1.25 · I_sc · N_p) [A], String fuse

### PV yield & economics

*Annual yield, self-consumption, savings, payback* — `pv_yield`

```text
E = P_peak · specific yield    Saving = E · self-consumption · energy price    Feed-in = E · (1 − SC) · tariff
Payback (simple) = investment / annual benefit – without interest, degradation, maintenance.
Specific yield e.g. from PVGIS: Germany typ. 850–1100 kWh/kWp, Swiss plateau approx. 950–1100 kWh/kWp, Alps higher.
```

**Inputs:** System size [kWp], Specific yield [kWh/kWp], Self-consumption share [%], Grid energy price [€ / CHF], Feed-in tariff [€ / CHF], Investment [€ / CHF], Grid CO₂ factor [g/kWh]

**Results:** Annual yield [kWh], Thereof self-consumed [kWh], Self-consumption saving [€], Feed-in revenue [€], Benefit per year [€], Payback (simple) [a], CO₂ saving per year [t]

### PV DC cable: losses

*Power loss and minimum cross-section of string cable* — `pv_dc_cable`

```text
P_loss = 2 · L · I² · ρ / A    Loss % = P_loss / (V_mpp · I) · 100    A_min = 2 · L · I · ρ / (v · V_mpp)
Common target: DC loss ≤ 1 %. Conductor temperature 70 °C (roof). Check ampacity of the solar cable (H1Z2Z2-K, EN 50618) in the datasheet.
```

**Inputs:** String voltage V_mpp [V], String current I_mpp [A], Cable length (one way) [m], Cross-section, Permitted loss [%]

**Results:** Power loss [W], Loss [%], Minimum cross-section for target [mm²], Energy loss per year (≈ 1000 full-load hours) [kWh]

### Battery storage sizing

*Capacity from night consumption and rules of thumb* — `pv_storage`

```text
C_nominal = E_night / (DoD · η_discharge)
Rules of thumb (e.g. HTW Berlin, homes): approx. 1.0–1.5 kWh storage per 1000 kWh annual consumption and no more than approx. 1.5 kWh per kWp. Larger batteries add little autarky.
```

**Inputs:** Evening/night consumption [kWh/d], Usable depth of discharge [%], Discharge efficiency [%], Annual consumption [kWh], PV size [kWp]

**Results:** Nominal capacity for night demand [kWh], Rule of thumb consumption, Upper limit by PV size [kWh]

## Building, fire safety & acoustics

### Fire safety & escape routes

#### Escape route: width & length (ASR A2.3)

*Minimum widths by number of persons, maximum route length* — `escape_route`

```text
ASR A2.3 (edition 03/2022, amended 2024), section 5: Tab. 1 minimum widths (column B doors/passages, column C main escape route): up to 5 p. 0.80/0.90 · 20 p. 0.90/1.00 · 50 p. 0.90/1.20 · 100 p. 1.00/1.20 · 200 p. 1.05/1.20 · 300 p. 1.65/1.80 · 400 p. 2.25/2.40 m; above 200 persons linear interpolation allowed. For new buildings 0.90 m is recommended for doors up to 5 persons (accessibility).
Main escape route length (straight line): 35 m (normal, or increased fire hazard with extinguishing system), 25 m (increased fire hazard without), 10 m (explosive substances); actual walking distance ≤ 1.5 times.
The book (ASR A2.3:2007) still has one column: 0.875 / 1.00 / 1.20 / 1.80 / 2.40 m and 20 m for toxic and explosive areas. Building law may allow longer rescue routes.
```

**Inputs:** Persons in the catchment area, Room type, Escape route length (straight line) [m], Actual walking distance [m], Existing clear corridor width [m]

**Results:** Minimum width main escape route [m], Minimum width doors/passages [m], Max. escape route length [m], Max. walking distance [m]

#### Escape route widths ASR A2.3

| Persons | Doors/passages 2022 | Main route 2022 | Escape route 2007 |
|---|---|---|---|
| up to 5 | 0,80 m (Neubau 0,90) | 0,90 m | 0,875 m |
| up to 20 | 0,90 m | 1,00 m | 1,00 m |
| up to 50 | 0,90 m | 1,20 m | 1,20 m |
| up to 100 | 1,00 m | 1,20 m | 1,20 m |
| up to 200 | 1,05 m | 1,20 m | 1,20 m |
| up to 300 | 1,65 m | 1,80 m | 1,80 m |
| up to 400 | 2,25 m | 2,40 m | 2,40 m |

*ASR A2.3, edition 03/2022 (GMBl. 2022 p. 227, amended 2024), section 5 Tab. 1. The 2007 edition is in the book (folder fire protection & workplaces). Check the edition in force at BAuA.*

#### Lift shaft: smoke vent & ventilation

*Free area of the shaft opening per MBO or VKF* — `lift_shaft`

```text
DE – MBO § 39 (3): smoke vent opening ≥ 2.5 % of the shaft floor area, at least 0.10 m² (check the state building code). Ventilation per DIN EN 81-1/-2 (2010): recommended 1 % of the shaft floor area at the top.
CH – VKF fire protection guideline (sheet 2007): clear area 5 % of the shaft cross-section, max. 1600 cm²; machine/pulley room vented separately at least 500 cm².
Sources: ZVEI/VDMA guide on smoke venting and ventilation of lift shafts (07/2012), p. 3; IG-BSK sheet D_AE_001 (03/2007). EN 81-1/-2 are replaced by EN 81-20/-50; check the editions in force.
```

**Inputs:** Shaft width (inside) [m], Shaft depth (inside) [m]

**Results:** Shaft floor area [m²], Free area smoke vent [m²], Free area smoke vent [cm²], Ventilation (recommended 1 %) [m²]

### Acoustics

#### Reverberation time (Sabine) & DIN 18041

*Actual and target reverberation time, absorber area needed* — `reverberation`

```text
Sabine: T = 0.163 s/m · V / A,   A = α · S (equivalent absorption area)
Target reverberation time DIN 18041 (occupied room, mid frequencies, tolerance ±20 %):
A1 music T = 0.45·lg V + 0.07 · A2 speech/lecture T = 0.37·lg V − 0.14 · A3 teaching/communication T = 0.32·lg V − 0.17 · A4 inclusive teaching T = 0.26·lg V − 0.14 (A4 since DIN 18041:2016).
Source: BAuA research report Fb 1030 “Lärm in Bildungsstätten” (2004), ch. 3, p. 19–20 (Tab. 3.1: 150 m³ → 0.53 s); on p. 50 the signs of the constants are misprinted. Check against DIN 18041:2016.
```

**Inputs:** Room length [m], Room width [m], Room height [m], Use (DIN 18041), Mean absorption coefficient of surfaces, Absorption coefficient of absorbers, Existing absorber area [m²]

**Results:** Room volume [m³], Reverberation time actual [s], Reverberation time target [s], Tolerance upper limit (+20 %) [s], Equivalent absorption area actual [m²], Absorption area needed [m²], Additional absorber area [m²]

#### Add sound levels & distance

*Energetic sum of sources, decrease with distance* — `sound_levels`

```text
L_Σ = 10 · lg Σ 10^(L_i/10)   (two equal sources: +3 dB)
Point source in the free field: L₂ = L₁ − 20 · lg(r₂/r₁)   (double distance: −6 dB)
Acoustics basics; cf. BAuA Fb 1030 “Lärm in Bildungsstätten” (2004).
```

**Inputs:** Level source 1 [dB], Level source 2 [dB], Level source 3 [dB], Level source 4 [dB], Measuring distance [m], New distance [m]

**Results:** Total level [dB], Total level at new distance [dB]

## Communication & pinouts

### Network & telephone

#### Pinout RJ45 / Ethernet

| Pin | T568A | T568B | 10/100BASE-T | 1000BASE-T | PoE mode B |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | white-green | white-orange | TX+ | BI_DA+ | – |
| 2 | green | orange | TX− | BI_DA− | – |
| 3 | white-orange | white-green | RX+ | BI_DB+ | – |
| 4 | blue | blue | – | BI_DC+ | DC + |
| 5 | white-blue | white-blue | – | BI_DC− | DC + |
| 6 | orange | green | RX− | BI_DB− | – |
| 7 | white-brown | white-brown | – | BI_DD+ | DC − |
| 8 | brown | brown | – | BI_DD− | DC − |

*TIA-568, IEEE 802.3. Patch cable: same on both ends (usually T568B). PoE mode A uses pairs 1/2 and 3/6.*

#### Ethernet with PoE

| Pin | T568B colour | Data (10/100) | PoE mode A | PoE mode B |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | white-orange | TX+ | DC+ / DC− | – |
| 2 | orange | TX− | DC+ / DC− | – |
| 3 | white-green | RX+ | DC− / DC+ | – |
| 4 | blue | unused | – | DC+ |
| 5 | white-blue | unused | – | DC+ |
| 6 | green | RX− | DC− / DC+ | – |
| 7 | white-brown | unused | – | DC− |
| 8 | brown | unused | – | DC− |

*IEEE 802.3af/at (PoE, PoE+): mode A feeds over data pairs 1/2 and 3/6 (phantom), mode B over spare pairs 4/5 and 7/8 (10/100 Mbit/s). IEEE 802.3bt (4PPoE, up to 90 W) uses all four pairs. Polarity in mode A depends on MDI/MDI-X. Power at the device: af 12.95 W, at 25.5 W, bt type 3 51 W, type 4 71.3 W.*

#### RJ-9, 11, 14, 25, 48 pinout

| Jack | Contacts | Assignment |
|---|---|---|
| RJ9 (4P4C) | 4 | 1 mic, 2/3 receiver, 4 mic (vendor-specific) |
| RJ11 (6P2C) | 6 | 3 ring, 4 tip (line 1) |
| RJ14 (6P4C) | 6 | 3/4 line 1, 2/5 line 2 |
| RJ25 (6P6C) | 6 | 3/4 line 1, 2/5 line 2, 1/6 line 3 |
| RJ48C (8P8C) | 8 | 1 RX ring, 2 RX tip, 4 TX ring, 5 TX tip, 3/6 shield |

*Registered jacks (FCC Part 68 / TIA-968): RJ11 = 6P2C (1 line), RJ14 = 6P4C (2 lines), RJ25 = 6P6C (3 lines), RJ9 = 4P4C (handset cord), RJ48C = 8P8C for T1/E1. Contacts from the left looking at the contacts, latch down. Tip / ring of one line are symmetrical to the centre.*

#### Fibre optic colour code

| Fibre | Colour TIA-598 |
|---|---|
| 1 | blue |
| 2 | orange |
| 3 | green |
| 4 | brown |
| 5 | slate |
| 6 | white |
| 7 | red |
| 8 | black |
| 9 | yellow |
| 10 | violet |
| 11 | rose |
| 12 | aqua |

*Fibre and buffer tube colours per TIA-598-D (common internationally; DIN VDE 0888 partly uses another order: red, green, blue, yellow, white, grey, brown, violet, turquoise, black, orange, pink). From fibre 13 repeated with ring/stripe. Jackets: OS1/OS2 yellow, OM1/OM2 orange, OM3 aqua, OM4 aqua or violet, OM5 lime green. Connectors: UPC blue, APC (8°) green, multimode beige/aqua.*

### Fieldbus & industry

#### RS-485: Modbus RTU / BACnet MS/TP

| Line | Modbus | Common names | Function |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | B / B' | B, +, Data+, TxD+/RxD+ | non-inverting line |
| D0 | A / A' | A, −, Data−, TxD−/RxD− | inverting line |
| Common | C / C' | GND, 0 V, SC | reference (do connect!) |
| Termination | 120 Ω | at both ends | between D1 and D0 |
| Screen | – | SH | to PE per EMC concept |

*Modbus over Serial Line V1.02: D1 = B/B' (positive in idle state "1"), D0 = A/A'. Note: many manufacturers swap the A/B naming – if in doubt measure idle polarity (D1 > D0).*

#### Pinout RS-232 (D-sub 9)

| Pin | Signal | Direction (DTE) | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | DCD | input | Data Carrier Detect |
| 2 | RxD | input | Receive Data |
| 3 | TxD | output | Transmit Data |
| 4 | DTR | output | Data Terminal Ready |
| 5 | GND | – | Signal Ground |
| 6 | DSR | input | Data Set Ready |
| 7 | RTS | output | Request To Send |
| 8 | CTS | input | Clear To Send |
| 9 | RI | input | Ring Indicator |

*TIA-232, male connector on DTE (PC). Null modem: 2↔3, 7↔8, 4↔6, 5–5.*

#### Pinout M12 A-coded (sensor)

| Pin | Colour | Function | IO-Link |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | brown (BN) | L+ (24 V DC) | L+ |
| 2 | white (WH) | output 2 / NC | DI/DO |
| 3 | blue (BU) | L− (0 V) | L− |
| 4 | black (BK) | switching output Q1 | C/Q |
| 5 | grey (GY) | manufacturer-specific / FE | – |

*IEC 61076-2-101, wire colours per IEC 60947-5-2; IO-Link port class A.*

#### IO-Link pinout

| Pin | Port class A | Port class B |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | L+ (24 V) | L+ (24 V) |
| 2 | I/Q (DI/DO) | P24 (actuator +24 V) |
| 3 | L− (0 V) | L− (0 V) |
| 4 | C/Q (IO-Link / SIO) | C/Q (IO-Link / SIO) |
| 5 | not connected | N24 (actuator 0 V) |

*IEC 61131-9 (IO-Link). M12 A-coded. Class A: pin 2 free (DI/DO), class B: pins 2/5 additional galvanically isolated actuator supply. Unshielded 3- or 5-core cable, max. 20 m. Wire colours: 1 brown, 2 white, 3 blue, 4 black, 5 grey.*

#### KNX bus cable

| Core | Function |
|---|---|
| red | bus + (KNX+) |
| black | bus − (KNX−) |
| yellow | spare / auxiliary supply |
| white | spare / auxiliary supply |

*KNX TP1, bus cable e.g. J-Y(St)Y 2×2×0.8 or KNX-certified (green sheath).*

| A1 | GND | B12 | GND |

### Audio

#### XLR (audio/DMX) pinout

| Pin | Audio (3-pin) | DMX (3/5-pin) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Ground / shield | Common / shield |
| 2 | hot (+) | Data 1 − |
| 3 | cold (−) | Data 1 + |
| 4 | – | Data 2 − (optional) |
| 5 | – | Data 2 + (optional) |

*Balanced audio per IEC 60268-12 / AES14 (pin 2 = hot). DMX512 per ANSI E1.11 (USITT): 5-pin XLR standard, 3-pin common; RS-485, 120 Ω cable and termination at the end.*

#### Jack (TRS/TRRS) pinout

| Contact | Mono (TS) | Stereo (TRS) | Balanced (TRS) | Headset CTIA | Headset OMTP |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Tip | Signal | left | hot (+) | left | left |
| Ring 1 | – | right | cold (−) | right | right |
| Ring 2 | – | – | – | ground | microphone |
| Sleeve | ground | ground | ground / shield | microphone | ground |

*Tip, ring, sleeve. TRRS headset: CTIA/AHJ (common today: Apple, Android) vs. OMTP (older devices) – microphone and ground swapped.*

#### MIDI pinout

| Pin | Signal | Description |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | NC | not connected |
| 2 | Shield | ground at MIDI OUT only |
| 3 | NC | not connected |
| 4 | Current source | +5 V via 220 Ω |
| 5 | Current sink / data | via 220 Ω |

*MIDI 1.0 (MMA): 5 mA current loop with an opto-isolator in the receiver, 31 250 bit/s. Shield (pin 2) connected to ground at the sender (MIDI OUT) only.*

*Through-hole LED (3/5 mm): long leg = anode (+), short leg = cathode (−), flat side of the body = cathode. RGB LED with 4 legs: longest leg = common (anode or cathode). Series resistor see calculator. Typical forward voltages at 20 mA see table.*

### Antenna & CCTV

#### Antenna length

*Wavelength and length of dipole, monopole (λ/4) and 5/8 λ* — `antenna_length`

```text
λ = c / f (c = 299 792 458 m/s)    l = fraction · λ · k
k = shortening factor (wire dipole ≈ 0.95, thick tubes smaller); λ/2 dipole: total length, each arm l/2.
```

**Inputs:** Frequency f [MHz], Antenna type, Shortening factor k

**Results:** Antenna length [cm], Wavelength λ [m], Length without shortening [cm]

#### CCTV storage and bandwidth

*Bitrate, network load and storage for video surveillance* — `cctv_storage`

```text
Bitrate ≈ pixels · frames/s · bits per pixel (guide values: H.264 0.08, H.265 0.04, MJPEG 0.75 bit/pixel; strongly scene-dependent – enter the manufacturer value if known).
Bandwidth = n · bitrate    Storage = n · bitrate · recording time · share / 8 (1 TB = 10¹² bytes).
```

**Inputs:** Number of cameras, Resolution, Frames per second [1/s], Video codec, Bitrate per camera (manufacturer) [Mbit/s], Recording per day [h], Share recorded (motion) [%], Retention [d]

**Results:** Storage required [TB], Bitrate per camera [Mbit/s], Total network load [Mbit/s], Storage per day [GB]

| Name | Pixels | Megapixels | Aspect |
|---|---|---|---|
| QCIF | 176 × 144 | 0.03 | 11:9 |
| CIF | 352 × 288 | 0.1 | 11:9 |
| 4CIF | 704 × 576 | 0.41 | 11:9 |
| D1 (PAL) | 720 × 576 | 0.41 | 5:4 |
| 960H | 960 × 576 | 0.55 | 5:3 |
| 720p (HD) | 1280 × 720 | 0.92 | 16:9 |
| 960p (1,3 MP) | 1280 × 960 | 1.23 | 4:3 |
| 1080p (Full HD, 2 MP) | 1920 × 1080 | 2.07 | 16:9 |
| 3 MP | 2048 × 1536 | 3.15 | 4:3 |
| 4 MP | 2560 × 1440 | 3.69 | 16:9 |
| 5 MP | 2592 × 1944 | 5.04 | 4:3 |
| 6 MP | 3072 × 2048 | 6.29 | 3:2 |
| 4K UHD (8 MP) | 3840 × 2160 | 8.29 | 16:9 |
| 12 MP | 4000 × 3000 | 12 | 4:3 |

*Common resolutions of surveillance cameras (analogue: CIF/D1/960H, IP: HD to 4K). Storage see calculator “CCTV storage”. “MP” names are vendor conventions, exact pixel counts may differ.*

## Conversion & units

### Electrical quantities

#### Voltage

*Units, RMS/peak/peak-to-peak (sine) and dB levels* — `conv_voltage`

```text
Sine: Û = √2 · U_rms    U_pp = 2 · Û    rectified mean |ū| = 2Û/π = 0.9003 · U_rms
Levels (RMS): dBV = 20 · lg(U / 1 V), dBu = 20 · lg(U / 0.7746 V), dBmV = 20 · lg(U / 1 mV), dBµV = 20 · lg(U / 1 µV).
```

**Inputs:** Value, Unit, Given as (sine)

**Results:** RMS value [V], Peak value Û [V], Peak-to-peak [V], Rectified mean [V], RMS value [mV], RMS value [kV], Level [dBV], Level [dBu], Level [dBmV], Level [dBµV]

#### Power

*W, kW, PS, hp, BTU/h, kcal/h, TR* — `conv_power`

```text
Conversion via the SI base unit. Factors per ISO 80000 or common definitions (PS = 735.49875 W, hp = 745.6999 W, kcal = 4186.8 J, BTU (IT) = 1055.056 J).
```

**Inputs:** Value, Unit

**Results:** watt [W], kilowatt [kW], megawatt [MW], metric horsepower [PS], mechanical horsepower [hp], BTU per hour [BTU/h], kilocalorie per hour [kcal/h], ton of refrigeration [TR]

#### Energy / work

*J, Wh, kWh, kcal, BTU* — `conv_energy`

```text
Conversion via the SI base unit. Factors per ISO 80000 or common definitions (PS = 735.49875 W, hp = 745.6999 W, kcal = 4186.8 J, BTU (IT) = 1055.056 J).
```

**Inputs:** Value, Unit

**Results:** joule [J], kilojoule [kJ], megajoule [MJ], watt-hour [Wh], kilowatt-hour [kWh], megawatt-hour [MWh], kilocalorie [kcal], British thermal unit [BTU]

#### var / µF conversion

*Reactive power of a capacitor ↔ capacitance* — `conv_kvar_uf`

```text
Q = ω · C · U_C² per capacitor, ω = 2πf.
1~: U_C = U. 3~ star: U_C = U/√3, Q_tot = 3 · ω · C · U²/3 = ω · C · U². 3~ delta: U_C = U, Q_tot = 3 · ω · C · U² (U = line voltage, C = capacitance per capacitor).
```

**Inputs:** Given, Reactive power Q (total) [kvar], Capacitance per capacitor [µF], Voltage U [V], Frequency, Connection

**Results:** Capacitance per capacitor [µF], Total reactive power [kvar], Current (line) [A], Voltage across capacitor [V]

#### Angles and trigonometric functions

*Degrees, radians, gon ↔ sin, cos, tan (also phase angle cos φ)* — `trig_angle`

```text
φ [rad] = φ [°] · π/180    φ [gon] = φ [°] · 400/360
sin, cos, tan, cot of the angle; from sin/cos/tan the principal value is returned (arcsin −90…90°, arccos 0…180°, arctan −90…90°). Power factor: cos φ = P/S, tan φ = Q/P.
```

**Inputs:** Value, Given

**Results:** Angle [°], Angle [rad], Angle [gon], sin, cos, tan, cot

### Conductor sizes

#### AWG ↔ mm²

*American Wire Gauge to metric cross-section* — `conv_awg`

```text
d = 0.127 mm · 92^((36 − AWG)/39)    A = π/4 · d²
Enter AWG 1/0 = 0, 2/0 = −1, 3/0 = −2, 4/0 = −3.
```

**Inputs:** Given, AWG, Cross-section [mm²]

**Results:** AWG, Diameter [mm], Cross-section [mm²], Next metric standard size ≥ [mm²]

#### Cross-section / area

*mm², cm², m², in², kcmil (MCM), cmil* — `conv_area`

```text
Conversion via m². 1 in = 25.4 mm (exact); 1 cmil = area of a circle of 1 mil (0.001 in) diameter = π/4 · (25.4 µm)² = 5.0671 · 10⁻⁴ mm²; 1 kcmil (MCM) = 1000 cmil = 0.5067 mm².
```

**Inputs:** Value, Unit

**Results:** square millimetre [mm²], square centimetre [cm²], square metre [m²], square inch [in²], square foot [ft²], kilo circular mil (MCM) [kcmil], circular mil [cmil]

### General quantities

#### Length

*mm, m, km, mil, in, ft, yd, mi, nmi* — `conv_length`

```text
Conversion via metres. Inch 25.4 mm, foot 0.3048 m, yard 0.9144 m, mile 1609.344 m, nautical mile 1852 m (all exact by definition).
```

**Inputs:** Value, Unit

**Results:** micrometre [µm], millimetre [mm], centimetre [cm], metre [m], kilometre [km], mil (1/1000 inch) [mil], inch [in], foot [ft], yard [yd], mile [mi], nautical mile [sm]

#### Temperature

*°C ↔ K ↔ °F* — `conv_temperature`

```text
T[K] = θ[°C] + 273.15    θ[°F] = θ[°C] · 9/5 + 32
```

**Inputs:** Value, Unit

**Results:** Celsius [°C], Kelvin [K], Fahrenheit [°F]

#### Pressure

*Pa, kPa, bar, mbar, psi, mWS* — `conv_pressure`

```text
Conversion via the SI base unit. Factors per ISO 80000 or common definitions (PS = 735.49875 W, hp = 745.6999 W, kcal = 4186.8 J, BTU (IT) = 1055.056 J).
```

**Inputs:** Value, Unit

**Results:** pascal [Pa], hectopascal [hPa], kilopascal [kPa], millibar [mbar], bar [bar], pound per square inch [psi], metre of water column [mWS], millimetre of mercury [mmHg]

#### Flow rate

*m³/h, l/s, l/min, CFM, gpm* — `conv_flow`

```text
Conversion via the SI base unit. Factors per ISO 80000 or common definitions (PS = 735.49875 W, hp = 745.6999 W, kcal = 4186.8 J, BTU (IT) = 1055.056 J).
```

**Inputs:** Value, Unit

**Results:** cubic metres per hour [m³/h], cubic metres per second [m³/s], litres per second [l/s], litres per minute [l/min], litres per hour [l/h], cubic feet per minute [CFM], US gallons per minute [gpm (US)]

#### Rotational speed

*rpm, 1/s, rad/s, °/s* — `conv_speed`

```text
Conversion via rad/s: ω = 2π · n/60 (n in rpm); 1/s = Hz. Synchronous speed n_s = 60 · f / p (p = pole pairs) see calculator speed & slip.
```

**Inputs:** Value, Unit

**Results:** revolutions per minute [1/min], revolutions per second (Hz) [1/s], angular velocity [rad/s], degrees per second [°/s]

#### Currency € ↔ CHF

*Convert euro and Swiss franc* — `conv_currency`

```text
CHF = € · rate    € = CHF / rate    (rate = francs per euro)
Left empty, the rate from the settings is used (also for all cost results). October 2026 about 0.92 CHF/€ – use your bank's daily rate.
```

**Inputs:** Amount, From, Rate 1 € in CHF [CHF]

**Results:** Result, Rate used 1 € = [CHF], Inverse rate 1 CHF = [€]

### Units & prefixes

#### Units of measurement

| Quantity | Unit | Symbol | Definition |
|---|---|---|---|
| current | Ampere | A | SI base unit |
| voltage | Volt | V | 1 V = 1 W/A |
| resistance | Ohm | Ω | 1 Ω = 1 V/A |
| conductance | Siemens | S | 1 S = 1/Ω |
| charge | Coulomb | C | 1 C = 1 A·s (1 Ah = 3600 C) |
| capacitance | Farad | F | 1 F = 1 C/V |
| inductance | Henry | H | 1 H = 1 V·s/A |
| magnetic flux | Weber | Wb | 1 Wb = 1 V·s |
| flux density | Tesla | T | 1 T = 1 Wb/m² |
| frequency | Hertz | Hz | 1 Hz = 1/s |
| active power | Watt | W | 1 W = 1 J/s = 1 V·A |
| apparent power | Voltampere | VA | S = U · I |
| reactive power | Var | var | Q = U · I · sin φ |
| energy | Joule | J | 1 J = 1 W·s; 1 kWh = 3.6 MJ |
| electric field strength | Volt pro Meter | V/m | E = U / d |
| magnetic field strength | Ampere pro Meter | A/m | H = I · N / l |
| luminous intensity | Candela | cd | SI base unit |
| luminous flux | Lumen | lm | 1 lm = 1 cd·sr |
| illuminance | Lux | lx | 1 lx = 1 lm/m² |
| temperature | Kelvin | K | SI base unit; θ [°C] = T − 273.15 |
| force | Newton | N | 1 N = 1 kg·m/s² |
| pressure | Pascal | Pa | 1 Pa = 1 N/m²; 1 bar = 10⁵ Pa |

*International System of Units (SI, ISO 80000 / IEC 80000). Prefixes see table SI prefixes, conversions see converters.*

#### SI prefixes

| Prefix | Symbol | Factor |
|---|---|---|
| Tera | T | 10¹² |
| Giga | G | 10⁹ |
| Mega | M | 10⁶ |
| Kilo | k | 10³ |
| Hekto | h | 10² |
| Deka | da | 10¹ |
| Dezi | d | 10⁻¹ |
| Zenti | c | 10⁻² |
| Milli | m | 10⁻³ |
| Mikro | µ | 10⁻⁶ |
| Nano | n | 10⁻⁹ |
| Piko | p | 10⁻¹² |

*ISO 80000-1 / SI brochure.*
